# Dustline development tracker

This is the master backlog for improving the existing game. Preserve working features. Inspect the actual project before deciding what is missing. A menu label is not evidence that a feature works.

## Status rules

- UNVERIFIED: Requested or proposed; implementation has not been inspected.
- TODO: Confirmed missing.
- IN PROGRESS: Currently being implemented.
- BLOCKED: Needs a specific external dependency or decision; record it.
- DONE: Implemented and verified; record the evidence.
- DEFERRED: Deliberately postponed; record why.

Initial entries began UNVERIFIED. The 2026-09-23 inspection below now records individual findings; untested behavior remains UNVERIFIED. Original requirements should remain in scope. Suggested additions and proposed quantities/designs may be refined after inspection and playtesting.

For each milestone, record status, existing implementation, remaining work, verification, and blockers. Update this file after each task. Do not mark a milestone DONE while required checklist items remain incomplete. Track partial completion at item level.

## Known context

- Game title shown: Dustline — Broken Signal.
- Screenshot shows Story, Skirmish, Private Co-op, and four classes: Assault, Marksman, Support, Medic.
- Screenshot shows a mission involving a convoy route log, radio relay, and extraction.
- User reports rapid sprint rubberbanding, insufficient visual quality, and unclear or repetitive gameplay objectives.
- Updated inspection: Build 04 implements unlimited sprint and fixes two reproduced movement-timing defects. Current human play, GPU performance and live network play remain UNVERIFIED. Saves and a game-account backend are confirmed absent.
- 2026-09-23 human playtest (user, real browser, Build 04): sprint slowed and the view shook after a few seconds; MOV-01/M1.02 were reopened and the F3 overlay added. Later 2026-09-23 user playtest: "sprint jitter is gone in Safari on Build 04". M1 acceptance confirmed by the user; the specific cause was not isolated (no overlay dump was supplied).
- Build 05 user playtest (2026-09-23), verbatim: "fall direction is correct but every death plays the same animation; blood is too small and only visible up close; there is no blood on surfaces; the kill marker change is unwanted; a downed ally appears to end the mission. No stutter observed — frame rate held up in combat."
- Build 06 user playtest (2026-09-23), verbatim: "blood scale and scoped blood are good. Wall splats could not be assessed because enemies never stand near walls. The six death variants still feel practically identical to me in play. FPS drops below 60 during sustained fire but not severely. One enemy is stuck in a wall to the right and straight ahead from the player spawn."
- Build 08 user playtest (2026-09-24), from the user's cleanup request: "I played Build 08 and the deaths still look the same to me. Authored variants are not achieving what I want." Bug: "an enemy actively fighting me turned away mid-fight and went back to patrolling." Decisions: keep the six prop materials (visual quality; the frame budget has room); no money on assets (free route only: Microsoft Rocketbox, MIT, with Mixamo animations); hybrid ragdoll (M2.06 option 2) chosen, to be built after Ambush mode; **Ambush (F.03) is the next milestone**.
- **Co-op bug report (user, 2026-09-28):** on the live site, two players on different networks, both on Build 14 after a hard refresh, could not connect: pasting the code and pressing JOIN WITH CODE gave "Wrong connection code. Use the matching offer or answer from DUSTLINE." every time, in both directions, using COPY CODE.
- Build 15 (2026-09-28; written locally, since published and played by the user on two networks): **COOP-01 fixed, co-op handshake only.** The rejection was code handling, not the network: that message could only mean an answer code where a host code was expected or the reverse, and the game's own buttons led there in two ways. Codes now name their kind and length and carry a checksum, a pasted code is handled by what it is whichever button is pressed, the code box is emptied while a new code is prepared, every wrong input has its own message, and a connection that fails after a correct exchange is reported as a network failure. No gameplay, Story, Skirmish or Ambush change. T25.
- **User report (2026-09-28):** "my friend and I connected successfully on Build 15 over the public site, on different networks, with no relay." COOP-01 is confirmed fixed by play, and **a TURN relay is not currently needed** (NET-01 stays filed for networks that cannot connect directly).
- **Standing decision (user, 2026-09-28): Ambush never has AI squadmates, in any configuration. In Ambush the only teammate is ever a real player.** The Build 17 option to turn a squad on in Ambush is removed; the AI squad choice remains for Story, Skirmish and Story co-op.
- **Standing decision (user, 2026-09-28): co-op must be fair between host and guest. Neither player may have an advantage from which machine is hosting.**
- **Two-player playtest (user and a friend, 2026-09-29, on the live build, the first time co-op was played by two people):** (1) "When a game ends, my friend is kicked out of the lobby and we have to exchange codes again." (2) "My friend reports enemy bullets doing 10 damage each." (3) "Friendly fire with the precision rifle: one body shot did 99 damage." (4) "Neither of us could revive the other. A downed player was simply dead." What each turned out to be is in E32; (1), (3) and (4) are changed in Build 20, (2) is as intended.
- **User verdict on the look slice (2026-09-29): approved.** "It is significantly better than Kohar Valley. Build the map this way."
- Build 31 (local, 2026-10-01, **not pushed**): **stair width and glass corners.** Every stair flight is 2.4 m (2.39 to 2.51 clear; the user asked for wider a fourth time, after the eye fix made the stairs good to run: widened, not investigated again). The customs house's wells are 5.1 m wide and take the house's depth beside the corridor: half landings 3.45 and 4.85 m, entries 4.84 m, roof doors 2.4 m (2.19 clear); **the layout paid for it with the six closets, their doors and twelve windows, and .8 m off four rooms a storey**; wider would need a bigger house (E47). **The whole opening of a window is the pane to a shot** (post to post, sill to head, and the post of a double window): 2,538 shots at nine places on every window break their pane. **The teeth of glass are gone**: nothing is left in a broken opening; four drawings. Build 30's eye untouched (text pinned). Enemies on a path are nudged past what the path clears by a centimetre. 283 panes. **Kohar Valley identical** (traces). 34 suites, 304 checks; T41 new (7). B27 awaits the user. Found, not fixed: MAP-11.
- Build 30 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**): **window frames and the eye on stairs.** Step A (E46): the stairs were called tight at 1.37, 1.7 and 2.0 m and no dimension was the cause; the eye took every tread as a 21 cm jump in one frame, 10 times a second walking. The eye is now eased over the treads at the feet's own rate (worst frame 4 cm walking, 8 sprinting; never more than .3 m behind; jumps, falls, pull-ups, ladders, the feet, the speed and the field of view unchanged). **A broken window is a hole:** the casement, the iron bars and (with both halves gone) a double window's post go with the pane, from the picture, the kit's list and the body's space; the reveal, lintel, sill, shutters and short teeth of glass stay; a barred window shot out can be climbed through. Five drawings for all the windows. **Kohar Valley identical** (traces; its eye where it was). 33 suites, 297 checks; T40 new (6). B26 awaits the user. Found, not fixed: MAP-10.
- Build 29 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**): **windows and stairs on Dehrun Terraces.** Step A measured why 1.7 m stairs felt tight (E45): not the width but bodies stopping dead against anything proud of a wall (17 of 23 flights), half landings 1.37 m clear and roof doors about 1.1 m. Every flight is now 2 m (1.99 to 2.12 clear), the customs house's half landings 2 m and roof doors 1.9 m, and the player's moves are nudged past anything up to 20 cm proud of a wall (21 dead stops on the flights before, none now). Windows 1.4 by 1.3 m, 46 double windows, the town's shells glazed: 294 panes. **Glass can be shot out:** a whole pane stops sight and bullets, the breaking shot stops there, a broken pane stops nothing and stays broken for the mission; grenades, blasts and bodies break it; enemies and squadmates shoot out a pane they see someone through; the host decides, the guest is told and checks every snapshot. Three drawings for all the glass, nothing made after load. **Kohar Valley identical** (no glass there; traces). 32 suites, 291 checks; T39 new (9). B25 awaits the user. Found, not fixed: MAP-09.
- Build 28 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**): **the layout of Dehrun Terraces (map build 7 of 9): the whole town in grey-box, stairs for two, Skirmish on it.** Six terraces from the lower town (−1.6 m) to the top of the hill (6.4 m), lanes with steps at x 0 and ±48 and sheer retaining walls between, districts of one- to three-storey shells either side of the block on every terrace (43 shells; nine open houses with a door, a room and an outside stair to the roof), the square with the customs house in the middle, town walls all round, eleven open gateways; most of it from a generator in `dist/map-dehrun.js`. Every stair flight is 1.7 m (the customs house's 1.77 in 3.6 m wells; house A's inside stair 1.7): a player and an enemy stand side by side on all 23. Skirmish plays on the map (start on the block's top terrace, the relay on the square, seven enemies on the square and the lower town, reinforcements through the gates, `leave` loop per map); Story there is still the walk. Space rules for stairs: a tread is brushed only within .66 m of the feet; the mover walks down steps; a tread only brushed at the origin is not stood against. The enemies' navigation covers the town (92,914 places); T38's floods find nothing stranded and no place without a way back. **Kohar Valley identical** (traces). 31 suites, 282 checks; T38 new (6). B24 awaits the user. **Not built: changing map inside a running page (item 4 of the brief; MAP-01 stands, plan in MAP-08).** Found, not fixed: MAP-08.
- Build 27 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**): **co-op height (map build 6 of 9) and the customs house widened.** Host and guest see each other at the right height on stairs, ladders, roofs and mid-fall (positions always travelled as three numbers); a guest's fall is charged by the host from the guest's poses (`remoteHeight`), as the host's own; revive reach is three-dimensional on maps with height (both pages and the host's acceptance); `mateNow()` keeps the teammate's height (a blast under a floor used to find it on the terrain); connected on a look map the host runs the AI; knife, blast, grenades and the guest's shots were already three-dimensional. The customs house: corridor 3 m, rooms 44 to 143 m² (closets aside), bare doorframes inside (1.6 m from the corridor, 1.3 m between rooms), leaves kept on the four street doors. **Kohar Valley identical** (traces; T37 replays a scripted two-player run against Build 26). 30 suites, 276 checks; T37 new (8). B23 awaits the user. Found, not fixed: MAP-07.
- Build 26 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**): **enemy height on Dehrun Terraces (map build 5 of 9).** On a map with a space the enemies walk a navigation in layers (`dist/navmesh.js`, made by the kit: a flood by a body of their width under their own stair rule, doorway places, one-way drops ≤ 3 m, ladders both ways; 19,304 places in ~120 ms at load), reach every floor and the roof of the customs house from the street, climb both stairs both ways and the block's ladders, pass every door, see, shoot and take cover across levels (cover per level), fall with the player's fall damage and blood where they land, are pushed by shots (a body pushed off within 3 s is the player's kill). `?foes=1` on the look map puts six foes in the customs house and on the square. **User decisions:** the block's doors widened to 1.3 m and house A's inside stair to 1.05 m; parapets and sills unchanged (cross-level fights are through windows and stairwells); fall kills the player's within 3 s of a push. **Kohar Valley identical:** no navigation there; cover table, twelve paths, spawn spots and the fire block the same as Build 25 run from its commit (T36), the three traces in their suites. 29 suites, 268 checks; T36 new (8). B22 awaits the user. Found, not fixed: E42.
- Build 25 (local, 2026-09-30, **not pushed**; B21 measured by the user: the customs house walked at 2.6 to 2.8 ms a frame, Kohar Valley unchanged at 3.4): **kit interiors and the customs house (map build 4 of 9).** The kit builds rooms (partitions along every edge two rooms share, doors on them, a plain way into each stair), dog-leg stairs with a half landing and a railing of posts under a sloping handrail, and stair heads onto the roof, all from the map's description. The customs house (house K) stands on a new walled square south of the block's gate, which now stands open: 26 × 18 m, three storeys (3.4, 3.2, 3.2 m) and a roof at 9.8 m with a .6 m parapet; a corridor east–west through every storey, 24 rooms in all (closets, halls, a store, a loft), two stairs in opposite corners from the ground to the roof, doors to the street on all four sides, every door and doorway 1.3 m wide (E41), furnished with the light models only. The space learned five rules on the way (seams, holes, stepping down, sliding out of an overlap, being pushed clear on landing; nothing thinner than .2 m holds a body up), all found by the new flood check and the roof fall. **Kohar Valley identical (T31, the three traces); Build 24's kit and description replayed from `8e92bf3`: the new kit makes the very same boxes from the old description.** 28 suites, 260 checks; T35 new (8). - **Build 24 published 2026-09-30** at the user's request: `3dbb944`, `89dc5de`, `8e92bf3` and the documentation correction `a60906b` pushed together. - Build 24 (2026-09-30, **live**): **player height on Dehrun Terraces (map build 3 of 9).** On a map built of boxes with tops and bottoms the body has a real height (`dist/space.js`: radius .34, standing 1.8, crouched 1.1, steps up .35, pulls itself up 1.35): it walks stairs and steps tread by tread with its feet on the stone, steps onto crates, is stopped by barrels; jumps .7 m and falls; falls hurt from 3 m and kill at 12 (100 × ((h − 3) / 9)^1.5: 6 m 19, 9 m 54); Space pulls it onto a ledge ahead when there is room to crouch on it (roof parapets, the retaining wall's parapet, the shop counter, windows without bars, crate stacks) and jumps otherwise; three ladders (walk into one to climb, back onto it from the top, Space lets go, nothing fires, stabs or throws on a ladder); crouching under low things (the workshop's shutter is half down) and held down while under them; walking off any roof, balcony or terrace edge falls; the map's edges hold at every height; the M map says what the body stands on and how high. The open house got a stair well, an upper room and a roof hatch with a ladder. **Kohar Valley:** no space, its flat lines are Build 23's text, a jump moves the camera alone; T1, T20, T26, T31 pass. 252 checks in 27 suites; 34 of 34 breakages caught. **Not measured in Safari: bench URLs given to the user.** Reports E39 (inescapable places) and E40 (the kit and the Ambush arena).
- **Builds 22 and 23 published 2026-09-30 (UTC; the evening of 2026-09-29 for the user)** at the user's request: `6bca1dc`, `c1098b6`, `157d1fe` and `0b6aca5` pushed together. GitHub run 36652816925: `check` (stamp and all 26 suites, about 14 minutes) and `deploy` succeeded. The live page says BUILD 23 with build token `7f8e1ae1dd02`, the local stamp; the new map's description, kit, loader, a model, a surface picture and the sky each answered from the live site with their full size. The block is at the live address with `?map=dehrun`.
- Build 23 (2026-09-29, **live**): **look slice critique and fixes.** Nothing flickers any more: 442 exposed places where two surfaces shared a plane are none (the largest was 151 m² each side of the street, the terrace's ground in the face of the retaining wall; most were in the kit and would have been in every block). Windows have a casement with glass and glazing bars set back in the wall; shut doors stand in their openings with battens and a handle; rolling shutters are slats in guides. Awnings are woven, striped cloth in colours the block has, sagging, with a hem, on a rail and a pole carried by posts or brackets. Beam ends differ in spacing, length and thickness; balconies are propped. Surfaces carry their own shade: uneven over metres, darker at the foot of what stands upright. The sun in the sky stands where the light comes from; shade is lit. Yards are trodden ground with paving along the house fronts. Twelve plain houses and 51 lengths of field wall stand beyond the walls. 91 draw calls, about 341,000 triangles from the street (ceiling 350,000: no room left). 26 suites, 244 checks; 40 breakages caught. Safari not used.
- Build 22 (2026-09-29, **live**, published with Build 23): **the look slice of Dehrun Terraces (map build 2 of 9).** One street block of the second map, to walk and look at: three terraces of a cobbled street that climbs north by two flights of stone steps, eight houses of one to three storeys (stone ground storeys, plaster above, beam ends, shutters, balconies, parapets, awnings), three of them open on the ground floor (a furnished house, a shop with its counter and rolling shutter, a workshop), dry-stone retaining walls, yards, a lean-to, two gates, wires, lamps, and 71 props of 28 kinds. Reached by the address `?map=dehrun`; no mission, nobody else there. Built by a kit (`dist/terraces.js`) from a description (`dist/map-dehrun.js`); 15 photographed surfaces, 28 models and a sky from Poly Haven (CC0), and the Three.js model loader (MIT): **28.7 MB added, cap 30 MB; the game is 45 MB, of which Kohar Valley still fetches 16**. Everything drawn: 92 draw calls and about 305,000 triangles (ceilings 500 and 350,000), counted in the desktop app's browser. **Kohar Valley fetches nothing of the new map** and is Build 20's world, number for number (T31, T32). 25 suites, 238 checks; 45 deliberate breakages caught. **Safari: not measured for the block (B18); stopped at the user's word.** Height is not built: upper floors, outside stairs, balconies and roofs are geometry.
- **Build 21 published 2026-09-29** at the user's request, at the second attempt. **First push (`715b2be`): GitHub run 36627399090 failed** in `check`, at the new suite T31 ("differs from Build 20 at cover.hash"); `deploy` was skipped and Build 20 stayed live. Cause: a fault in the test, not in the game. T31 compared the world byte for byte with a record stored from the developer's Mac; GitHub's runner is a Linux machine with Node 24.21 (the Mac has 24.19), and a number differed in its last digits there. **Fix (`868fb12`, tests only, by the user's decision):** T31 takes Build 20 whole from its commit and runs it on the spot (`tests/old-build.mjs`), and compares with that; the stored record is deleted. The five suites that compared Story and Skirmish with the stored Build 09 trace (T20, T21, T22, T23, T27) were of the same kind (a record from the Mac, though rounded to 1 mm, which is why they had always passed on GitHub) and now compare with Build 09 taken from its commit `6560f7a` and run on the spot; whether the stored trace agrees is reported (`storedRecordAgrees`, true on the Mac), never required. No other suite depends on a stored record (`sprint-before-m1.json` is evidence only and is not loaded). **Second push: GitHub run 36631040830 succeeded** (`check` and `deploy`); the live page says BUILD 21 with build token `824b7e080dcd`, the local stamp. No file in `dist/` changed between the two pushes.
- Build 21 (2026-09-29, **live**): **Kohar Valley as map data (map build 1 of 9).** Nothing visible changed. Kohar Valley is map 1, described in `dist/map-kohar.js`: the ground, sky and sun, the edges, the navigation grid, every house, wall, sandbag line, pole, stall, crate, truck, tree and prop, the scatter ranges, the relay, the route log and the extraction, every start, the enemy posts, patrol loops and reinforcement entries, and the Ambush arena (start, first area, lowered walls, areas, barricades, crates, what the map shows, the spawn lattice). `dist/maps.js` holds the maps and the active one; the game builds its world from it at load, and the Ambush rules, the enemy rules and the props follow it. Proof: the world built from the map is Build 20's number for number (27 parts, T31), every moved list equals the Build 20 sources, the three recorded traces replay (Story and Skirmish against Build 09, solo Ambush against Build 15), 411 values changed in turn each change the game, and a second small map built from the same kind of description is deployed and played in all three modes. 24 suites, 233 checks; 125 deliberate breakages caught (the 67 altered map values caught again after the test fix). Safari: no difference from Build 20 (B17). **Not built:** changing map inside a running page (MAP-01).
- **Build 20 published 2026-09-29** at the user's request (GitHub run 36607988021: `check` and `deploy` succeeded; the live page says BUILD 20 with build token `772dc9b40a85`, the local Build 20 stamp). Two commits went up: Build 20 (`7a9bb82`) and the report-only filing of E33 (`e9288a2`).
- Build 20 (2026-09-29, **live**): **co-op fixes and revive.** The session outlives the mission in every co-op mode: nothing but DISCONNECT or a lost connection ends it, and L after a mission brings both players back to the same menu, still connected. Skirmish can be played by two (it could not: choosing it disconnected). A player whose health runs out in co-op is down, not dead: the teammate holds E within 2 m for 4 s to bring them back with 40 health, within 30 s; the mission ends when both are down at once. A player's head is as narrow as an enemy's, so a shot into the shoulder is no longer a head hit. Enemy damage is unchanged. Safari measured at last (B16): inside smoke costs nothing that can be measured. T30, E32, B16.
- **Build 19 published 2026-09-29** at the user's request (GitHub run 36517620756: `check` and `deploy` succeeded; the live page says BUILD 19 with build token `45e0c0915011`, the local Build 19 stamp).
- Build 19 (2026-09-29, **live**): **Equipment: a sidearm, a knife and three throwables.** Every class carries an M9 sidearm beside its rifle (Z changes weapon: .45 s to draw, 26 a hit, weaker with range), a knife on its own key (T: 2.3 m, 65 from the front, a kill from behind) and throwables (G, hold to cook a fragmentation grenade; Tab changes the item): fragmentation (170 within 2 m falling to nothing at 8 m, stopped by walls), smoke (a 5 m ball for 14 s that nobody sees through, enemies included) and flash (blinds whoever is looking at it, players and enemies alike). Story and Skirmish hand out 2 fragmentation, 1 smoke, 1 flash and three sidearm magazines at every deployment; in Ambush throwables (300 / 200 / 250 at wave 1) and sidearm magazines (30) are bought at the crates. In co-op the host decides all of it, and the guest's knife, throws, draws and reloads are judged by what the guest saw and counted from when the guest began them. Rifles are unchanged; Story, Skirmish and solo Ambush replay their recorded runs. An interrupted session (power loss) was resumed after a recovery check found the work intact. T29, E31, B15.
- **Build 18 published 2026-09-28** at the user's request (GitHub run 36508835987: `check` and `deploy` succeeded; the live page says BUILD 18 with build token `dad86c0945c0`, the local Build 18 stamp). It carries Build 17 with it.
- Build 18 (2026-09-28, **live**): **Ambush fairness and cleanup.** The Ambush squad option is gone. Every shot is judged against what its shooter was looking at (PVP-01), no further back than 300 ms. The guest's rifle, reload and dressing take as long as the host's on a delayed or jittery connection. Enemies look for the guest at the height they use for the host. Each wave's arrivals are dealt equally to the two players at any distance apart, each from the wave's direction as that player sees it. Either player's pause holds the mission, and the run waits for the guest to be in. The spectator camera stops short of walls. Solo Ambush still replays Build 15; Story and Skirmish still replay the Build 09 trace. T28, E30, B13, B14.
- Build 17 (2026-09-28, **live**, published with Build 18): **AI squad toggle** (its Ambush part was removed in Build 18). A box "AI squad" in the settings row beside Audio and Blood, one choice per mode. Untouched, every mode is what it was (three squadmates in Story, Skirmish and Story co-op, none in Ambush). In co-op the host's choice governs and the guest sees it; the real teammate is never part of it. Ambush with the squad on uses the existing two-player waves (no third curve). Turning the squad off in Story or Skirmish shows a one-line warning. At a co-op extraction a downed player now banks alongside the survivor (user decision from the Build 16 review). T27.
- **Build 16 published 2026-09-28** (GitHub run 36498264718 succeeded; the live page says BUILD 16 and carries build token `296f6ed6c408`, the local Build 16 stamp). **Build 16 has not been playtested with a real second player: the blue teammate's readability at combat distance and the two-player difficulty curve are both unverified.**
- Build 16 (2026-09-28, **live**): **Ambush co-op (F.11).** Two real players, never an AI squad. The host runs the waves, spawns, barricades, both players' points and every hit. Points are per player; a barricade either player clears is open for both; rifles, magazines and dressings are bought from the buyer's own points. A player who goes down watches the teammate and the run ends when both are down. Extracting takes both players' choice. Solo and co-op personal bests are kept apart. The teammate wears blue with a blue marker overhead, in every co-op mode. Friendly fire is on in co-op. NET-04 fixed (a short interruption holds the mission instead of ending it) and AI-03 fixed (the teammate is fired at like the host). Solo Ambush replays Build 15 sample for sample. T26, B12.
- **Published (2026-09-27):** the project lives at **https://github.com/alex-wilson141/Dustline** (public) and is served by GitHub Pages at **https://alex-wilson141.github.io/Dustline/**. Every push to `main` publishes automatically after the checks pass. The repository replaced a single "Add files via upload" commit of 23 September (the Build 04 baseline, 42 files, no shared history, nothing that was not also local). Before the first push the 13 local commits were rewritten to the GitHub no-reply address and the macOS username was removed from a file path in this roadmap, at the user's request; every commit ID changed (table under H2). The earlier public Site (chatgpt.site, an older build) is separate and was not touched.
- **Build 13 user playtest (2026-09-27):** "Build 13 put four separate signposts on screen at once — waypoint lines, the areas list, the minimap and the masts — and together they are clutter. The M map is a small circle in the corner with labels printed on top of each other; it is unreadable. The masts look wrong in the valley." Goal set by the user: fewer and better signals, not none; a new player must still find every barricade and crate.
- Build 14 (local, 2026-09-27): **Ambush map and signposting rework.** One signpost instead of four: M opens a full-screen, north-up map of the whole arena (areas open or closed, standing barricades with prices, crates with the rifle each sells, the arena edge, the houses, your position and facing), laid out so that no label overlaps another, covers a marker or leaves the canvas. The HUD waypoint lines, the area list and the signal masts are removed; the minimap is cut from Ambush (Story and Skirmish keep their M panel). In the world, every barricade carries amber paint, a painted price board and a lantern on its timber section, and every crate green paint, a board naming its rifle and a lantern; the lantern is lit while the thing can be bought. One radio line per barricade when it first becomes affordable points to the map. Area 4 renamed **North houses** (it lies north on the game's compass; the north-up map exposed the old name). No price or difficulty change. T24; B11. Not published.
- **Build 12 user playtest (2026-09-27), solo:** wave 8, 99 kills, 11m10s. "The difficulty curve is right, do not change it." Two problems: the third area's barricade could not be found, so its crate was never reached and only three of the four rifles were seen; 13,350 points earned against roughly 3,000 spent, "points accumulate with nothing to spend them on". **The solo curve (2 → 9) is confirmed good.**
- Build 13 (local, 2026-09-27): **Ambush navigation.** Step A facts: four rifles are for sale, one crate per area (CQB courtyard, carbine field office yard, DMR west lane, automatic rifle south houses); the rifle never seen was the DMR, behind the West lane barricade; nothing is missing or unreachable. Built: 12 m signal masts (amber at every barricade that can be bought now, green at every crate in an open area), two HUD waypoint lines with arrow and distance to the nearest of each, the M map's Ambush layer (standing barricades with prices, closed areas shaded and named, crates) and an area list, all read from one live state function. Report only: the economy of the run (E25); no price and no part of the difficulty curve changed. Filed, not started: co-op with real players only in every mode (F.13), friends-only PvP (F.14, blocked by AI-03). T23; B10. Not published.
- **Build 11 user playtest (2026-09-25):** "the AI squad kills most of the enemies, so I am not the one surviving and the difficulty curve does not apply to me. Ammunition ran low by wave 3 while my squad did the fighting. The crate stat comparison line is unreadable in combat. The bandage key is not discoverable."

## Provenance and future-task rules

- **ORIGINAL:** direct requests in the existing game conversation: first-person team combat in a fictional Afghanistan-like valley; more realistic visuals/mechanics; trackpad looking with hidden/captured cursor; four selectable loadouts; enterable buildings; richer routes; story and multiplayer; working reload/fire; animations; performance; a less crowded HUD and a way to leave. The latest direct M1 requirement is **unlimited sprint**: no stamina resource, meter, exhaustion, recharge delay, resource-based speed changes, jump restriction, aim penalty or class perk. Hold the sprint input with permitted movement to keep sprinting; retain the existing forward-input, aiming, crouching, firing, reload and healing gates. This supersedes any earlier stamina design or exhaustion/recovery acceptance plan.
- **PLAN:** future scope in the supplied roadmap and pasted milestone plan: expanded equipment/classes, new menu tabs, configurable bots, rooftop waves, progression/cosmetics, accounts/cloud saves and campaign/maps. These are tracked requirements for later bounded tasks, not authorization to implement all of them now. M1 is governed by the updated ORIGINAL requirement above.
- **SUGGESTION:** entries explicitly called “Suggested”/“Proposed” in the master roadmap. In conflicts, the master roadmap's provisional status wins: the exact eight-class roster/roles, operation names, three-by-six mission count, 15–25-minute target and environment list are not verified or immutable designs. Do not silently convert them into original requirements.
- **SETUP:** the user's initial inspection/tracker request. Mixed labels preserve original scope while identifying later additions.
- After **every development task**, read this file first, preserve current working features and Broken Signal story content, work on only the selected milestone and necessary prerequisites, then update affected item statuses/evidence and append a progress-log row: date, source revision, files/features changed, checks and results, unverified acceptance, remaining work, exact blockers and next task.
- `DONE` means the named scope was checked; a code test is not a claim of visual quality, real networking or human playtesting. Checkbox `[x]` is reserved for DONE. For partial items, retain an allowed status and state the completed subpart plus remaining work. At this handoff, M1 code work is complete and human acceptance is UNVERIFIED; no checklist feature remains IN PROGRESS. DEFERRED preserves scope and records why it is postponed. A missing feature is TODO; do not invent an external BLOCKED state just because it is not built yet.
- Do not replace established lore with the pasted plan's proposed occupied-city premise without an explicit story decision. Keep Kohar Valley, Viper squad, the relief convoy, route log, relay and extraction.
- Desktop screen/audio-access testing was stopped after the user objected. M1 verification uses headless code checks; no desktop recording was requested. Gameplay does not need screen recording, microphone or camera permission. Leave real pointer capture/visual feel UNVERIFIED unless tested through an acceptable method or confirmed by the user.

## Project baseline and evidence register

**Project:** the current repository root.
**Live game:** https://dustline-mountain-front.smart-heron-4139.chatgpt.site/  
**Technology:** static HTML/CSS and JavaScript ES modules; bundled Three.js **r169** with custom rendering, X/Z collision, AI/pathfinding, mission loop and WebRTC. No React, build step, physics engine, dedicated gameplay server or save backend was found. The GitHub handoff adds a private ES-module `package.json` with test commands only; it has no dependencies. `dist/` is the authored runtime as well as the static hosting directory; do not assume it is disposable generated output. It is approximately 16 MB on disk.

| Evidence | What was inspected / result |
|---|---|
| E1 — project | Git tracked-file inventory, `README.md`, `.openai/hosting.json`, `dist/index.html`, and `dist/three.module.js:6`; clean starting checkout at the commit above. No applicable AGENTS.md found in checked ancestors/project. |
| E2 — modes/UI | `dist/index.html`; `game.js` `setClass`, `setMode`, `updateDeploy`, `start`, `pause`, `goMenu`, `reset`, keyboard/pointer handlers. Three real mode paths and four class configurations; not the future multi-tab shell. |
| E3 — story/results | `game.js` `interact`, `advanceStory`, `missionTick`, `hudObjective`, `finish`, `radio`. Hardcoded route-log recovery, 25-second uncontested relay accumulation, 5-second extraction; both humans must extract in co-op. Skirmish uses 45 seconds. No campaign clock deadline despite briefing prose; no checkpoints/rewards. |
| E4 — networking | `dist/network.js` `PeerSquad` and `game.js` `receive`/`networkTick`: manual offer/answer codes, real data channel, host-driven AI/health/mission, guest actions and remote positions, snapshots about every .08s, disconnect/pause handling. Three AI allies and seven enemies remain. |
| E5 — movement, updated Build 04 | `game.js` `blocked`, `move`, `frame`, `stepSimulation`, input/reset handlers; `timing.js`; `viewmodel.js`. Unlimited sprint retains normalized input, per-axis X/Z collisions, analytic ground height and existing combat gates. Simulation steps are no larger than 1/60s, with at most .25s catch-up per rendered frame. FOV/recoil/weapon pose use time-based damping; bob fades when stopping, weapon base position no longer accumulates bob/recoil, and jump integration is consistent across step sizes. No stair/platform/mantle controller was added. Historical .05s cap and resource-threshold oscillation are reproduced in T7. |
| E6 — combat/classes/bots | `dist/combat.js`; `game.js` `shoot`, `hitScan`, `reload`, `tickAI`, `sound`, healing, effect cleanup. Primary hitscan plus four loadouts, headshot threshold, per-class damage/armor/speed, self-dressings, LOS/A*, three squad orders. No multi-slot equipment, grenades, bot ammo/reload or cover planner. |
| E7 — art/performance | `dist/environment.js`, `characters.js`, `viewmodel.js`, `optimization.js` and renderer setup. Twelve building definitions with doors/windows/furnishings; one valley with side routes/cover. PBR photographs/HDR and adapted rifle model; procedural human rigs/hands/props. Static batching, bounding boxes, cached shadows, adaptive DPR and throttled HUD/AI exist. |
| E8 — licenses | `dist/credits.html`, `THREE-LICENSE.txt`, bundled assets. Credits record Poly Haven ground/plaster/brick/HDR and OpenGameArt M4A1 as CC0; Three.js is MIT. Unused legacy rough-plaster maps remain bundled. Recheck license provenance when importing new assets. |
| E9 — absence inventory | Inspected all authored runtime modules, HTML and project tree; searches for save/profile/auth/checkpoint/unlock/equipment/wave/difficulty systems matched no implementation beyond current run state, labels/descriptions and listed systems. No auth SDK, local persistence, database bindings or server routes. |
| E10 — sprint-slowdown mechanisms (2026-09-23) | `game.js` `frame`/`stepSimulation`/`applyQuality`/`move`, `timing.js`. Mechanisms that can change apparent speed, FOV or resolution during a held sprint: (1) **Auto render-scale ratchet** (`frame`, Auto only): every 5s of accumulated time, if the game's smoothed FPS < 42 the pixel ratio drops .15 (1.1 → .95 → .8 → floor .75) and never rises again during the session (menu reselection resets it); each drop resizes the canvas. (2) **.25s cap** (`timing.js`, all modes): discards simulation time only for a single frame > 250ms; frames up to 250ms are substepped at ≤1/60s with no time loss. (3) **Sprint gate** (all modes): Shift + W with no aim/crouch/held trigger/reload/dressing; losing it drops 6.1 → 3.5 m/s × class and FOV target 76 → 70 together. (4) Class, dressing (.45) and aim (.68) multipliers. (5) FOV target: aim zoom / 76 sprint / 70, damped. (6) Wall sliding in `move`. (7) Head bob ~1.9 Hz, visible as judder at low FPS. (8) Substep count scales with frame time; each substep runs player, viewmodel, all actor animation and AI tick, so slow frames cost more CPU (possible feedback loop). Found, not fixed: the smoothed FPS driving (1) is a per-frame 4% EMA of *uncapped* frame time, so one long frame (e.g. 1s) holds it under 42 for roughly 40 frames; the 5s timer also accumulates in the menu, so the first check can run on the first playing frame. Preview observation: game-smoothed FPS read 4.8 while measured FPS was a steady 60 after a page reload. At a 60 Hz display, 16.7ms frames alternate 1 and 2 substeps (ceil rounding). None of this is yet tied to the user's symptom. |
| E11 — hit/death propagation before M2 (2026-09-23) | `game.js` `hitScan`, `shoot`, `tickAI`, `receive`, `networkTick`; `characters.js` `animate`. Solo: player hits are real raycasts (headshot = hit > 1.45m above feet, 110 damage); kills `kills++` and set an instant `rotation.z = π/2` plus +.2m; surface hits make dust. Bot hits are chance-based (no ray/hit point); ally deaths roll and stand up after 15s. Host: same, plus guest `shot` messages raycast on the host, replying with a bare `hit`. Guest: received only that bare `hit` and 12.5 Hz snapshots whose HP forced `rotation.z = π/2` every packet; no hit events, tracers or dust (even for its own shots). Build 05 resolves the guest gap with host-sent `impact` messages (NET-03). |
| E12 — why a downed ally "ended" the mission (2026-09-23) | Step A workflow (two independent agents, real `tickAI` headless). Only 6 `finish()` callers: local player death, co-op human teammate (remote avatar) death, skirmish win, extraction win, connection close, host `end`. **No AI-ally path**: 18 × 240 s real-AI runs with 50 natural ally deaths, 0 failures; the skeptic found no counterexample. Both death cases printed "A squad member was lost…", so the player's own death read as an ally's. When an ally drops, its attacker retargets the player (measured 9→0 hits on the ally, 0→6 on the player). The 15 s "stand-up" teleports the ally to the regroup point ((index−1)·4, 65) behind the start, not where it fell. Build 06 names the victim ("You were killed." / "Your co-op teammate was killed…", guest gets its own point of view); AI rule unchanged. |
| E13 — breakable props feasibility (2026-09-23) | Investigation only; see M5.09. 14 candidates (10 crates, 4 market stalls with 72 fruit + 12 trays). Constraints: props merged by `batchStatic` (cannot hide individually), static shadow map (refresh per break), 2D `blocked()` solids, cached nav grid (0–2 cells per prop), host-authoritative co-op, `reset()` has no world restore, terrain raycasts ~3.6 ms. Prototype of option B (spheres vs analytic terrain + AABBs, rolling, sleep): 72 fruit settle in 2–3 s at 0.08 ms/step. |
| E14 — decal surfaces (2026-09-23) | 271 occluders: terrain mesh (64,800 tris, ~3.7 ms per downward ray — excluded from decals), 96 wall segments, lintels, sills, roofs, 12 floor slabs (terrain covers 57–100% of each), furniture, low walls, sandbag covers, brick walls, 10 crates, 4 counters, truck bodies, relay box; all unrotated/unscaled boxes so a face is the world AABB. A wall is within 2.5 m behind only ~2–4% of outdoor hits (29% indoors), so floor splats behind the victim carry the effect outdoors. Floor height uses the exact rendered terrain triangle (`terrainAt`, ≤1.5e-7 m vs mesh raycast). |
| B1 — M2.05 blood/decal frame cost (2026-09-23, Build 06) | Real game code and Kohar Valley scene in the Claude app's Chromium pane on the user's MacBook Pro M1 (ANGLE Metal), AC power, drawing buffer 1584×990 (1440×900 × 1.1 Auto), 5 targets at 15–40 m, synthetic 60 FPS clock, GPU timer queries + 1-px readPixels sync, ABBA Blood on/off, 2 × 480 frames per arm. Blood-on arm starts with decals at the 40 cap. **40 decals, no hits:** +1 draw call, GPU +0.009 ms. **8.6 hits/s (solo Assault):** 289 live particles, +4 draw calls, GPU +0.057 ms, whole frame +0.05 ms. **22.5 hits/s (co-op):** 762 particles, GPU +0.067 ms, frame +0.06 ms. **47.6 hits/s (stress):** 1,216 particles, GPU +0.069 ms (p95 +0.083), frame +0.03 ms. First hit after idle +0.3 ms (Build 05: 4–6 ms program-recompile hitch). Real automatic fire: Blood on vs off indistinguishable (noise ~0.25 ms). Headless CPU: spray 6 µs, decal placement 29 µs, ring add <1 µs, pool update ~11 µs/step. Build 05 comparison (Step A): ~0.57 draw calls per hit/s, +0.2 ms at 47.6 hits/s. Not measured: Safari, long sessions, lower-end GPUs. Scripts: scratch `build06/bench/dl-bench6.js`. |
| E15 — death variants in play; ragdoll scoping (2026-09-23) | Step A (headless real AI, real `shoot()` with spread/recoil, 372 player kills, 87 bot kills). Player kills: stagger 75%, collapse 20%, spin 4%, pitch 1%, doubleover and crumple 0%; bot kills stagger 100%. Causes: enemies turn instantly to their target, centre-mass aim never reaches the lower/leg zones, each variant replays identical keyframes, and every fall recedes along the shooter's line of sight (at the 37 m median, 0 px sideways and ≤10 px drop in the first .3 s); 19% of player-kill falls pass through cover. See M2.06. |
| E16 — Escape, Safari fullscreen and pointer lock (2026-09-23) | From WebKit-7621.2.5.11.8 (= the installed Safari 18.5) `EventHandler::internalKeyEvent`: Escape first releases pointer lock, then in element fullscreen cancels fullscreen and returns before the page's keydown; Chromium and Firefox also exit both. No page code can make Escape pause without leaving fullscreen. `document.exitPointerLock()` never touches fullscreen. Build 06 advertised Escape as pause in three places and P could not resume. Safari WebDriver remote automation is off; enabling it is a settings change, so real Safari was not driven. |
| E17 — PERF-02 cause (2026-09-23) | Confirmed: `visible()` and `hitScan` raycast `occluders`, whose first entry is the 64,800-triangle terrain mesh (no BVH); every downward ray tests all triangles (~3.8 ms headless). Realistic play ran ~45 such rays/s, mostly AI line of sight. A Build 07 CPU profile before the fix put 88% of time in terrain triangle tests via `visible()`. |
| E18 — AI-02 root cause (2026-09-23) | Positions were hand-typed and never validated: enemy spawn (12,−17) is 0.28 m inside the low wall (11,−17). Second cause: the 2 m nav grid sampled cell centres only, so ~1/3 of the solids `move()` collides with were invisible to routes; 121/300 random walks jammed (Build 06). Prototype with segment-checked links + validated placement: 294/300. |

Headless 150 s real-AI run: mean 4.1 → 0.08 ms, p95 18.8 → 0.17 ms, p99 31.7 → 0.36 ms, max 98 → 4.1 ms, with identical AI outcomes before and after. Not measured: Safari. |
| E19 — realistic characters and first-person hands: asset research (2026-09-24) | Research only (Build 08 Step A): nothing bought, downloaded, generated or integrated. **User decision 2026-09-24: no money will be spent on assets; only the free route remains — Microsoft Rocketbox (MIT) with Mixamo animations** (M5.03 characters, M5.04 hands). Licence is the binding constraint because a static web game serves its model files publicly: Rocketbox's MIT licence allows that with the licence text in the credits, and Mixamo animations are free with an Adobe account for use in projects (recheck the current Adobe terms before integrating). | E20 — stale module cache (2026-09-24) | In the Chromium pane, a normal load of `http://localhost:8765/` after the Build 08 edits ran the Build 08 `game.js` with the Build 07 `environment.js` from the HTTP cache (transferSize 0; 4,668-byte body = the Build 07 file), so the map had no props. `python3 -m http.server` sends Last-Modified without Cache-Control, so browsers reuse modules heuristically. A mix with a stale `enemy-ai.js` would throw in `reinforceTick` (no `waveSize`). Re-fetching the modules with `cache:'reload'` fixed it. Fixed by DEPLOY-01 (B4). |
| B3 — Build 08 frame cost: map density and crowds (2026-09-24) | Real game in the Claude app's Chromium pane on the user's M1 (ANGLE Metal), drawing buffer 1126×844 (pane size, Auto 1.1), synthetic 60 FPS clock, GPU timer queries + 1-px readPixels sync. With and without props in separate page loads of the same code (the no-props variant was the Build 07 `environment.js` as a temporary dot-file, since removed), order A B B A B A. The first A ran in a faster machine state (render CPU 1.3 ms in every scenario) and is excluded; the other five agree within 0.2 ms. **Static views** (6 vantage points across the village, 180 frames each, AI off): whole frame 8.88–9.06 → 9.50–9.61 ms (**+0.6 ms, ≈7%**), render CPU +0.6 ms, GPU 3.66–3.85 → 3.89–4.03 ms, draw calls 284.8 → 290.8 (+6, the six new prop materials; everything else merges into the existing static batches, 961 → 1,443 meshes merged), triangles 149,515 → 159,235. **Sustained automatic fire with real AI** (600 frames): 8.00–8.14 → 8.30–8.36 ms (**+0.25 ms, ≈3%**), p95 13.6–14.9 → 13.9–14.0 ms, game-logic CPU 0.71–0.74 → 0.85–0.87 ms (sight lines against 377 instead of 271 occluders), GPU 3.2–3.3 ms unchanged. **Crowds** (props, real AI, firing, 40 decals, one page load): 1 enemy in view 8.41 ms (p95 14.4, p99 18.5, 342 calls); all 7 enemies on screen as 5 corpses (the corpse cap) + 2 alive 8.71 ms (p95 13.6, p99 19.4, GPU 3.66, 411 calls); 7 alive (the alive cap) 9.20 ms (p95 16.0, p99 21.0, GPU 3.56, 495 calls). A corpse costs the same as a standing soldier (same meshes). Death-variant weights were retuned after these runs (animation choice only). Not measured: Safari, the user's full-window resolution, lower-end GPUs. Script: scratch `build08/bench/dl-bench8.js`. |
| B4 — DEPLOY-01 stale-cache check in Safari 18.5 and Chromium (2026-09-24, final code) | A caching test server (scratch `deploy01/cachesrv.py`, `driver.html`) served two builds, A then B. Every module carried a marker naming its build, and each page reported which build of every file actually ran. Each run loaded the page, switched the server to build B, then loaded the page again in a fresh frame (normal navigation). Five caching policies: (1) python http.server defaults with month-old files; (2) the same with `index.html`/`game.js` edited a minute before (the E20 case); (3) page revalidated, modules cacheable; (4) everything cacheable; (5) everything cacheable with `enemy-ai.js` evicted. Four pairings × five policies = 20 runs per browser. **Safari 18.5 (macOS 15.5, the user's browser) and the Chromium pane gave identical results.** **Build 08 scheme (control):** stale (all A) under (1) and (4); **mixed** under (2) (page and `game.js` B, 14 modules A), (3) (page B, 15 modules A) and (5) (page A, `enemy-ai.js` B). **DEPLOY-01 scheme:** all 15 modules and the page from build B in all five. **Upgrade** (cached Build 08 page, new server): never mixed; the new files' upgrade guard reloaded into build B under (2), (3) and (5); under (1) and (4) the cached Build 08 page kept running its own cached Build 08 files (consistent, until the page expires or is reloaded). **Downgrade** (cached new page, server back on an unstamped build): all B in all five. The server log shows the mechanism in both browsers: the cached page fetched the live page past the cache, replaced itself with `?build=<token>`, then every module arrived under the new token. Limits: builds differed in every file; Safari history loads (Back, restored tabs) and CDNs were not exercised. |
| E21 — AI-04 cause: engaged enemies dropping out of a fight (2026-09-24) | Reproduced with the real `tickAI`. (1) **Perception:** enemies sight-checked only the three nearest possible targets, so three allies nearer than the player but hidden from the enemy made a player in plain view at 28.5 m invisible: seen in 0% of frames (fixed: 99%). (2) **Engagement lifetime:** an engaged enemy gave up after `lostTargetSearch` (6 s) without its own sighting, even while its target was alive, 20–25 m away and firing; hearing refreshed the threat but not that clock, and its search ended in `resumeDuty` (patrol). (3) **Flapping:** after the first give-up, each heard shot re-armed the threat (hold), and the expired clock ended combat again on the next tick; in a 30 s test the enemy flipped between hold, search and patrol every frame (182 patrol entries, 701 state changes with the fix's clock reset removed). A 180 s free-play probe with real allies logged 105 exits from combat, all through the 6 s rule, 34 with all three allies nearer than the player. |

| E22 — Ambush Step A: rooftops and vertical movement (2026-09-24) | The 12 buildings are single-storey boxes from one `building()` function: flat roof slab at wall height (3.5–4.6 m) with a .42 m parapet and a water tank, 90–143 m² each. There are no stairs, ladders, hatches or upper floors; the nearest roofs are 10.4 m apart and most gaps are 12–15 m across open ground. `player.y = groundY(x, z)` every frame (jumping only offsets the camera), collision is a 2D footprint test and navigation is a 2D ground grid, so neither the player nor enemies can stand on a roof, and the co-op pose carries ground height only. A rooftop arena would need standing surfaces, stairs or ladders (forbidden without a separate request), bridges, multi-level navigation and co-op height sync (NAV-01, NET-02): roughly 8–12 dev-days before Ambush itself. The valley is otherwise open ground with detached buildings; the west district holds five buildings within ~60 m and the only near-enclosed space (the T-wall courtyard west of house B1). The user approved the ground arena proposed from this survey. |

| E23 — Terrain height variation and ground-surface variety, report only (2026-09-24, Build 10) | **Today:** the valley floor is `groundY(x,z) = .42·sin(.047x)·cos(.055z) + .14·sin(.13z)` (`game.js`): ±0.56 m over 115–135 m wavelengths, slopes under 2 %, a plain in practice. Everything derives from that one function: the 180×180 terrain mesh (64,800 triangles over 1100 m, 6.1 m cells), `terrainAt` (the same triangles in closed form), `terrainRay` (PERF-02, an exact cell walk of that mesh), every placement (`building()` and the 183 props sit at the ground height of their centre; cover points, edge markers and spawns likewise), player and actor heights (`p.y = groundY`; no slope speed, no fall damage; jump is a camera offset) and line of sight (`visible()`/`hitScan` already treat the terrain as an occluder through `terrainRay`). Collision (`blocked`, `move`, `segClear`) and navigation (91×91 cells of 2 m: `navigationGrid`, `safeSpot`, `pathTo`, the cover table) are strictly X/Z and never read height. **What slopes, berms and dips would take:** (1) a new height function, or an authored height map sampled through it, with the mesh, `terrainAt` and `terrainRay` staying derived from it, and a finer grid where shapes need it (a 2 m berm on 6 m cells is a pyramid); (2) a walkability rule for steep ground shared by collision and navigation (AI-02: navigation must match collision) — a gradient test through `terrainAt` normals inside `blocked`/`segClear`, or berm crests modelled as solids; (3) placement on slopes: a box sits at the ground height of its centre, so a 4 m crate on a 20 % slope floats 0.4 m at one end; buildings, walls, barricades, crates and props need per-corner sampling or flat pads, and the cover table's low/tall split (height above the centre point) becomes wrong along a sloping wall; (4) posture: soldiers stand upright at `groundY`, so slopes show feet floating or buried (visual only); (5) the PERF-02 equivalence test and T19/T20 spawn and reachability checks re-run against the new function; (6) Ambush spawn rules re-validated (spawns must be unseen: berms would help AMB-01). **Risk to enemy pathing:** A* and `segClear` are planar, so without (2) routes cross a 3 m berm as if flat and a dip can trap a route with `stuck` recovery only by side-stepping (the Build 10 watchdog exists only in Ambush); cover points and Ambush slots on slopes become hidden or exposed unexpectedly (an enemy 7 m away below a crest cannot see you and stands there); relay capture and extraction reachability must be re-proven. **Frame cost, measured headless on this Mac (Node 24, `terrainRaycaster`):** an exact terrain sight ray costs 1.8 µs on the flat 180² mesh, 2.5 µs with 2.5 m hills at 15–30 m wavelengths, 3.0 µs with those hills on a 360² mesh (259,200 triangles); with such hills 74–81 % of random valley sight lines are blocked by the ground, so the AI reaches far fewer targets per sense pass and hunts more. At a few hundred sight rays per frame this stays below 1 ms of CPU. GPU: a 4× denser terrain is unmeasured; it is one draw call that receives shadows, so the expected cost on an M1 is small but must be measured against the B6/B7 budget before adoption. **Estimate:** gentle relief (1–2 m over 30–60 m, no steep ground, placements re-sampled per corner) 2–3 dev-days; berms and dips as gameplay (slope rule, flat pads, re-validated routes and spawns, tests) 5–8 dev-days. **Ground-surface variety with bundled assets only** (the 18 files under `dist/assets`: `rocks_ground_05`, `beige_wall_001`, `rough_plaster_03`, `seaworn_sandstone_brick` diffuse/normal/roughness, one HDR, the rifle): (a) vertex colours on the terrain — the ridge mesh already uses `vertexColors`; tinting the floor by low-frequency noise plus height (lighter dust on crests, darker in dips, a trodden tone along the patrol loops and the market route) costs nothing per frame; (b) a canvas-generated macro map like the existing `texture()` speckle canvases as an `aoMap`/`lightMap` on a second UV set, to break the 520× tile repeat; (c) a second detail layer through `onBeforeCompile`, blending `rocks_ground_05` at two scales with `rough_plaster_03` roughness as dust patches (one shader edit, no assets); (d) more instanced clutter with the existing `pebbles` InstancedMesh pattern (gravel shoulders, rubble at wall bases; 500 instances today); (e) darker worn patches at route hot spots through the existing decal pool. Recommended order: (a), then (b), each measured against the frame budget; (c) only if repetition still reads in play. Nothing here was built. |

The rejected steeper curve (3 at wave 1, 9 from wave 13): σ 1° wave 4, σ 2.5° wave 2–3, σ 4° wave 2. A stationary bot in the open is a floor, not a forecast: a human moves, crouches behind the chest-high walls and buys dressings and magazines. Decision for the playtest: if wave 5+ is unreachable solo, lower `aliveCeiling` or raise the wave at which the cap grows; if too easy, restore the +2 curve. |

| E25 — Ambush economy of the user's Build 12 run, report only (2026-09-27) | **The run (user, Build 12, solo):** wave 8 reached, 99 kills, 11 m 10 s, 13,350 points earned, about 3,000 spent. **Where the income comes from:** a kill pays 100 and a headshot kill 150, so 13,350 = 99 × 100 + 69 × 50: 69 of 99 kills (70 %) were headshots, 135 points per kill on average, 35 % above the body-kill rate. Waves 1–7 hold 84 enemies and wave 8 twenty, so income per wave rose from about 810 (wave 1) to 2,700 (wave 8) and keeps rising by about 270 a wave to 5,400 from wave 18 (40 enemies). **Where points can go:** one-time purchases total 6,500 at most (barricades 750 + 1,000 + 1,250 = 3,000; the four rifles 500 + 750 + 1,000 + 1,250 = 3,500, of which a player needs none and wants one or two). Recurring purchases are a magazine (carbine 70 at wave 1, 120 at wave 8, 180 from wave 16) and a dressing (150, 260, 380). **Why the surplus:** (1) the player could not find the West lane barricade (1,000) or its crate (DMR 1,000), so 2,000 points of one-time purchases were unreachable (fixed in this build); (2) ammunition earns far more than it costs: a headshot kill takes one round and a body kill three, so at 70 % headshots a kill takes 1.6 hits; even at one hit in three that is about 5 rounds a kill, a 30-round magazine yields about 6 kills = 810 points and costs 70–120, under 15 % of what it earns, and the run started with 7 magazines (210 rounds, about 45 kills) before the first purchase; (3) dressings heal 50 for 150–260 and only a few are needed in 11 minutes; (4) recurring prices rise ×2.5 at most while income per wave rises ×6.7 (wave 1 to 18), so the share of income that upkeep takes falls as the run goes on: estimated upkeep for waves 1–8 is 1,000–1,400 (about 10 % of income), and at wave 18 about 1,300 a wave against 5,400 earned; (5) **points have no end value either way: the bank on extraction is `earned × multiplier`, not the unspent balance**, so spending never costs score and hoarding never helps. The 3,000 spent fits two barricades (2,000) plus about 1,000 of magazines and dressings. Had everything been found and bought (3,000 + three rifles 2,750 + upkeep 1,400 ≈ 7,150) about 6,200 would still have been left at wave 8. **What would make points a live decision (options, nothing changed here):** **A — bank the unspent balance instead of the points earned**, so every purchase trades score for survival; the strongest single change and it moves no price. **B — steeper upkeep**: a higher cap on the wave scale (×2.5 → ×4 or ×5) or a higher base, aiming at upkeep of 35–50 % of a wave's income late in a run. **C — repeatable sinks** beyond ammunition and dressings, for example rebuilding a barricade to close an approach for a wave, or armour; the classic sinks (mystery box, grenades, perks) are on the out-of-scope list and would need their own task. **D — income side**: a smaller headshot bonus or kill points that do not grow with the wave size. **E — dearer one-time purchases**: only delays the surplus. **Recommendation:** A first (it creates the decision on every purchase), then B tuned by playtest; C when new equipment is scheduled. |

The crate of the rifle you hold sells no rifle, so a run shows at most three for sale. The user's missing fourth rifle was the DMR, behind the West lane barricade (purchase point (−40, −8) on the west edge of the field office yard, 1,000 points). Every purchase point and crate has standing room inside the arena and a walking route from the start (probe and T23); the purchase point itself is in plain sight from 105 of 165 standing points of the yard, so it was a findability problem, not a broken one. |
| E27 — Step A: squad codes (F.12), report only (2026-09-28, Build 16 request; nothing built, signed up for or deployed) | **Goal (user):** CREATE SQUAD gives a 4-character code, the friend types it into JOIN SQUAD, both can then start Ambush together; the long-code exchange is replaced. **Infrastructure:** a rendezvous (signalling) service that holds the host's connection description under a short code for a few minutes, hands it to the joiner, and passes the joiner's answer back. It carries no game data. **Hosting, as read from the providers' pages on 2026-09-28 (through a summarising fetch; recheck before committing):** Cloudflare Workers Free 100,000 requests/day, 10 ms CPU per request, Durable Objects (SQLite) on the free plan with 100,000 requests/day, no idle sleep documented, paid from 5 USD/month; Workers KV unsuitable (1,000 writes/day, up to 60 s propagation, 60 s minimum expiry). Firebase Realtime Database Spark 100 simultaneous connections, 1 GB stored, 10 GB/month, no payment method, usable from the browser with security rules only (needs the SDK bundled and stamped). Supabase Free pauses after 1 week of inactivity. Render free spins down after 15 minutes idle, about one minute cold start. Fly.io no free allowance, card required. Railway 1 USD credit/month after a trial. Glitch shut down 2025-07-08. Deno Deploy Free 1M requests/month. PeerJS public server running, custom IDs allowed, no stated limits or guarantee, shared namespace. Trystero (public Nostr/MQTT/BitTorrent relays) active, no guarantee. **Relay (NET-01):** no primary figure for how often direct connections fail; secondary sources say roughly 15–25 % of consumer sessions need a relay (UNVERIFIED); causes are symmetric NAT, carrier-grade NAT, blocked UDP; an iPhone hotspot does symmetric NAT, so the advice in the Build 15 network-failure message may not help. Cloudflare Realtime TURN: 1,000 GB/month free as read from the pricing page (whether it applies to TURN without the SFU is not explicit), 0.05 USD/GB after, short-lived credentials minted server-side. At 50 kbit/s one relayed player uses 22.5 MB/hour. A relay can be added later without redoing the signalling: two entries in `iceServers` plus one credentials endpoint on the same service. **Found in the code:** Ambush co-op does not exist (F.11 not started; the AMBUSH tab is disabled while a session is connected), so the goal is F.12 plus F.11; the roadmap sequenced F.12 after F.11. NET-04 (transient `disconnected` ends the mission) will bite more often on real networks. **Recommendation given:** one Cloudflare Worker with a Durable Object, source kept in this repository outside `dist/`; long codes kept as the fallback when the service cannot be reached; relay credentials endpoint added to the same Worker once the first two-network attempt shows whether it is needed; Build 16 = squad codes for the existing co-op, Ambush co-op (F.11) as its own build after its two open design questions are decided. **Awaiting the user's decision.** |
| E28 — Step A: what Ambush co-op needs (2026-09-28, Build 16) | **Ownership:** the existing co-op already has the host run all AI, the mission and every hit (the guest sends its pose at 12.5 Hz, its shots and requests, and draws what the 12.5 Hz snapshots say), so the host owns waves, spawns, barricades, crates and both players' points. **Messages:** the Build 15 snapshot was fixed at 10 actors and carried no Ambush state, and the impact message refused actor numbers above 9; both are extended (any actor count; an `amb` block with wave, phase, timer, hostiles to come, bearing, waves survived, squad size, a barricade bitmask and for each player points, earned, kills, down, out-of-bounds time, extract choice, left), and four messages are new: `mode` (the host's menu choice), `buy` (E, B or N), `vote`, `ff`. The end message carries the run's numbers instead of a text, so each page writes its own report. **Pool:** Ambush has 16 enemy actors since Build 12 (the request said 20, which was Build 11); co-op needs 12 alive plus 4 bodies, so co-op uses 18 and solo keeps 16. **Found while reading:** every Ambush enemy hunted `player`, the host's own player, so a guest would have been ignored until seen; the host's player has no body a shot can hit; leaving the mission closed the connection in co-op, which with 1,400-character codes would have meant a new exchange after every run. **Connection drop mid-run:** any drop ended the mission (NET-04). **AI-03:** applies to Ambush co-op in full (chance .42 against .22, no crouch factor, damage 14–24 against 12–22 before armor), and is three expressions. **None of this changed the plan.** |

| E29 — What the squad toggle changes in the fight (2026-09-28, Build 17, report) | **Ambush with the squad on.** Build 11 had a squad in Ambush with its own curve (alive cap 4 rising to 12, pool 20) and the user's playtest found the squad doing most of the killing; Build 12 removed the squad and made the solo curve. Build 17 does not bring a third curve: with the squad on, Ambush uses the existing two-player waves (`waveSpec(n, 2)`: 9, 12, 15 … hostiles, 3 rising to 12 alive, arrivals a third faster) and the 18-enemy pool, which is close to what Build 11 used. Measured headless: the three squadmates alone, the player standing still and not firing, clear wave 1 (9 hostiles) in about 133 s. **Only the player's own kills count and pay (KILL-01)**, so every kill the squad takes is 100 points the player does not earn: with a squad the player is safer and poorer. In co-op with the squad on, two players and three squadmates face the same two-player waves; that will be easy. **Whether a third curve is needed is a playtest question; my recommendation is to try the two-player waves first and, if the squad carries the run, scale hostiles to 2 × solo for a squad rather than weaken the squad.** Solo and co-op curves and every price are unchanged. **Story and Skirmish with the squad off.** Both can be completed alone: nothing in the mission logic needs a squadmate (the relay counts the player, the extraction needs the player). Measured headless in Story, the player standing at the relay for three minutes: **damage taken 345 per minute alone against 220 with the squad (+57 %)**; at most three enemies fire at the player at once either way (attack tokens), so what changes is that nobody else draws fire. With 100 health and two dressings a player standing in the open there lasts about half a minute alone. Seven enemies plus reinforcements are the same in both cases, and alone the player must make every kill. **Recommendation, not built (mission design is out of scope): keep the missions as they are behind the warning; if solo Story is wanted as a real option, give it a solo setting that lowers the reinforcement budget and the alive cap, the same way Ambush got a solo curve.** |

| E30 — Audit: every difference between host and guest in co-op (2026-09-28, Build 18) | Read from the code and then tested from both sides (T28). **Fixed in Build 18:** (1) **Shot timing (PVP-01):** shots were judged on the host against the present, so the guest, who sees a picture made from snapshots, had to lead every moving target by its delay and the host did not. (2) **Rate of fire:** the guest's shots were accepted by arrival time, so shots that arrived bunched were refused: on a jittery connection the guest fired slower than the host with the same rifle. (3) **Reload and dressing:** both started when the message arrived and showed on the guest's page a snapshot later, so they took the guest the round trip longer than the host (about 0.2 s at 100 ms each way). (4) **What an enemy can see:** enemies looked for the guest at 1.25 m whatever its stance and for the host at 1.4 m standing and 0.75 m crouched, so a crouched guest behind a chest-high wall was seen and a crouched host was not. (5) **Who the enemies go for:** with equal numbers hunting each player the next arrival always went to the host; an enemy switched to whichever player it saw nearer, which sent most of a wave after the player on the side it came from; a wave's direction was chosen from the host's position alone; and when one player's ground offered no spawn point the wave waited for it (found by T28: one arrival in 170 s with the players in opposite corners). (6) **Pausing:** the host's pause held the mission for both, the guest's pause left the guest standing in the fight; and the run began while the guest was still on its capture screen. **Equal already, confirmed:** health and armor, dressings carried and their effect, damage taken per hit (since AI-03), points per kill and per headshot, every price, friendly-fire damage and body shape, the out-of-bounds rule, the extract vote, going down and spectating, what is banked. **Not equal and not changed (large, or a matter of who runs the simulation):** (a) the host's own health, ammunition and hit markers update at once, the guest's a snapshot and a trip later (up to about 0.1 s plus the delay): display only, the judging is equal; (b) if the host leaves or loses its connection the guest's run ends, if the guest does the host plays on: equal treatment needs the run to move to the other machine (host migration), which is large; (c) only the host can deploy, restart and choose the mode; (d) the guest reports its own position and the moment of its picture: both are bounded (8 m per pose, 1.7 m for a firing position, 300 ms for a picture) but a modified guest page could use those bounds, and a modified host page could do anything; between friends this is accepted; (e) in Story co-op the AI squad follows and guards the host's player, and orders from either player move the same squad. |

| T28 — Build 18 fairness between host and guest, and the spectator camera (`tests/test-fair-b18.mjs`, 2026-09-28) | 8 checks on two instances of the production game joined by a stand-in channel that delays every message by a number of frames each way, or holds and bunches them; every check is made from both sides. (1) The guest's picture is the host's world of a known moment: a walking enemy is drawn on the guest's page where the host had it at the moment the guest's shots name, mean error 2 mm, worst 5 mm, at 0, 50 and 100 ms each way and 2 and 3.5 m/s. (2) The same shot from host and guest at a walking enemy, each aiming at the middle of what that player sees: same damage, same zone, same pay, body and head, at those delays and speeds (the enemy had moved up to 1 m since the guest's picture). (3) Friendly fire at a strafing teammate: the same damage both ways, the victim's health the same on both pages. (4) The cap: claims of any age are judged no further back than 300 ms, future and broken claims now; a shot at where an enemy stood a second ago misses; a firing position 14 m from the guest is refused and one 1.3 m away accepted; beyond the cap (250 ms each way) the guest must lead. (5) The guest's rifle: twelve shots a fire interval apart all counted although nothing was delivered for 200 ms at a time, a flood of 300 messages a second held to the rifle's rate, reload and dressing taking the guest as long as the host within 0.05 s, an old reload claim credited no more than the cap; four rifles. (6) The audit: health and dressings, what an enemy sees (standing and crouched behind the chest-high wall, crouched in the open), damage taken, the prompt and the charges at a crate, either player's pause holding both, the host waiting for the guest at deployment, a lost connection releasing the hold, a solo page never held. (7) Arrivals at 3, 10, 25, 36, 45, 63 and 70 m apart, each with the players on either spot: never more than one arrival apart (for example 14 and 14, 16 and 16, 11 and 10), both hunted at least a third of the time, a spot hunted for a like share whoever stands on it, every arrival from outside the line, 35 m or more from both players, from the wave's direction as its own player sees it or, where that player's ground offers nothing, from any unseen direction (14 of 39 arrivals in the corner-to-corner case came from the teammate's side). (8) The spectator camera from 72 directions at seven spots for either player down: 3.4 m back in the open, as near as 0.84 m against walls, always with a clear line to the teammate's head. **Breakage pass:** 35 deliberate breakages of `game.js`, all caught on the first run. T27's list was rewritten for the new rule (28, all caught; one candidate was dropped because it changed nothing that could be seen) and T26's 60 were rerun against Build 18 with three patterns updated, all caught: 123 in all. **Found by the suite and fixed:** the guest's smoothed picture could only be known to several centimetres, so a guest's headshot at a running enemy missed where the host's hit: the guest now draws every character on the straight line between the last two snapshots, which the host can reproduce exactly; one player's ground without a spawn point stalled the whole wave; arrivals balanced by enemies alive gave the faster shooter more enemies (19 to 12), so each wave is now dealt by count. **Found and not fixed: AI-07.** **T27 revised:** its Ambush-squad check now asserts that no AI squadmate exists in Ambush under any setting, stored choice, host message, mode switch or reset, solo and co-op, and that nothing that placed, regrouped or briefed a squad in Ambush remains in the source. Headless: no real WebRTC, no packet loss, no changing delay. |
| E31 — Step A: what a second weapon, a knife and thrown items need (2026-09-29, Build 19) | **Today:** each player has one weapon state (rounds, reserve, reload, fire cooldown) configured from the class (`WeaponState`, `CLASSES`); firing is one instant ray (`hitScan`); the viewmodel is one rifle model scaled and dressed per class with a reload animation; nothing in the game flies. In co-op the host keeps the guest's weapon state and sends its rounds in every snapshot. **A second slot** needs a second weapon state per player (the host keeps the guest's too), which weapon is in hand, a draw time, a pistol in the hand (no asset exists, so it is built from boxes like the first rifle was), the weapon named in shot and reload messages, and the sidearm's rounds and the throwable counts in the snapshot. Impact messages need nothing. **A thrown item** needs no physics engine: position, velocity and gravity per step, with walls and ground found by the ray test bullets use (`solidOccluders`, `terrainRay`, `terrainAt` for the ground's slope), a bounce that keeps a third of the speed, and a fuse. **Area damage:** distance from the blast to each character's chest, and the existing clear-line test from the blast to chest or head for walls. **Smoke:** every enemy, squadmate, spawn and cover check goes through one function, `visible()`, so smoke blocks sight there; the old body of that function lives on as `clear()` for what smoke must not stop (blasts, the knife). **Flash:** facing is known for enemies (yaw) and the host (camera); the guest's pose carried yaw only, so pitch was added. **Constraint that shaped the code:** Story, Skirmish and solo Ambush must replay their recorded runs, so nothing may draw a seeded number or touch an enemy until a player uses an item. **Nothing changed the plan.** |

The bench was opened in Safari once (the user was warned first and told it needed about two minutes in front). As on 2026-09-28 it stayed behind: the desktop app was the frontmost application and the bench window was the second Safari window, so it never drew a frame and no sample exists. The attempt was ended rather than taking the screen. **What was measured instead, and is not a substitute:** the simulation alone, in Node, without drawing, same scenario, 3,599 frames: **0.15 ms mean, 0.24 ms p95, 0.67 ms p99, worst 1.7 ms per frame with four smoke clouds and 2.8 items in the air, against 0.11 ms mean without any** (7 enemies alive in both). The flight of the items, the smoke test in every sight check and the blasts cost 0.04 ms a frame. **What this does not show is the drawing:** four clouds are 36 large see-through sprites, and see-through surfaces that cover the screen are what costs a graphics card most; standing inside a cloud is the worst case. The pools are fixed (8 items, 4 clouds, 3 blasts), so the cost has a ceiling. The scene ran in the desktop app's Chromium pane without an error. To measure: the bench window on screen and in front for two minutes, no full-screen Safari window, display awake. |

| E32 — Step A: the user's four playtest findings (2026-09-29, Build 20) | **(1) The connection at the end of a game.** `goMenu()` closed the connection whenever the mode was Story co-op, after a win, after a loss and in the middle; in Ambush co-op it kept it, but only once the run had ended. Any mode tab other than PRIVATE CO-OP and AMBUSH closed it as well, silently. **Build 16 recorded COOP-02 as "returning to the menu after a co-op Ambush run has ended keeps the connection … Story co-op is unchanged (leaving closes it)": accurate, and Ambush only. It was not a regression; keeping the session in Story co-op had never been built.** Also found: **Skirmish co-op did not exist.** PRIVATE CO-OP is the Story mission; choosing SKIRMISH while connected disconnected the pair. **(2) Enemy damage.** Each hit is 12 to 22 before armor (`aiHit`, unchanged since the early builds, the same for host and guest since AI-03). By class: Support (armor .72) 8.6 to 15.8, mean 12.2, 9 hits to kill; Assault (.82) 9.8 to 18.0, mean 13.9, 8 hits; Marksman (.90) 10.8 to 19.8, mean 15.2, 7 hits; Medic (.95) 11.4 to 20.9, mean 16.1, 7 hits. "10 each" is the low end of that range for three of the four classes. **Intended; nothing changed.** **(3) The precision rifle on a teammate.** Friendly fire does the rifle's damage times the victim's armor: 78 to the body and 110 to the head before armor, as against enemies. 99 is exactly 110 × .9: **a head hit on a Marksman-class player**; a body hit on that player is 70.2. The rule was applied as written, but a player's hit shape was one box as wide at the head as at the shoulders (.52 m), where an enemy's head is .21 m wide, so a shot into the top of a shoulder or past the ear counted as a head hit. **Fixed: the shape, not the numbers.** **(4) Revive** did not exist: F.16, filed in Build 18 as the next co-op feature. **None of this changed the plan**; Skirmish co-op was added because "every mode" needs it. |
