Real game in **Safari** on the user's M1 (window 1440 × 820, canvas 1584 × 902, scale 1.1), scratch copy of Build 23 with the bench hooks (never in `dist/`), 3 × 600 frames after a 600-frame warm-up each. **Kohar Valley** (solo Ambush, wave 20, about 9 enemies alive, no throwables): main-thread frame work **3.27 / 3.20 / 3.03 ms mean, p95 5, p99 6 to 8, worst 8 (27 once)**; rAF interval 16.67 ms mean, p99 18; 258 draw calls, 164,000 triangles. **The block, standing at the start and turning** (every model in place, 117 to 119 draw calls, 350,000 to 356,000 triangles in view): **2.68 / 2.62 / 2.69 ms mean, p95 4, p99 4, worst 4 to 5**; rAF 16.67 ms, p99 18. **Walking the street's length** (68 to 117 draw calls, 190,000 to 346,000 triangles): **2.59 / 2.32 / 2.75 ms mean, p95 3 to 4, p99 4, worst 4 to 5**; rAF 16.66, p99 18. **Inside the open house** (97 draw calls, 311,000 triangles): **2.26 / 2.17 / 2.44 ms mean, p95 3 to 4, p99 4, worst 4 to 6**; rAF 16.66 in the clean run (the other two each had one gap of 8 and 11 s while the window was not visible; the frame work is unaffected). Start-up to the world built: Kohar Valley 317 ms; the block 329 to 458 ms (its models arrive afterwards). **Plainly: the block runs faster than Kohar Valley** (2.2 to 2.75 ms against 3.0 to 3.3 ms), at more than twice Kohar's triangles, with the 99th percentile at 4 ms against a budget of 16.7 and the screen holding 60 Hz throughout. What costs Kohar its time is the enemies (the AI runs on the main thread), not the scenery. **The 350,000 ceiling was set too low: the block uses about a quarter of the frame budget at it.** Recommendation, filed as the new ceilings for a block (T32 changed accordingly): **draw calls 700, triangles 700,000 in view**. Reasoning: doubling the block's triangles from here would, at the rate seen (about 0.4 ms per 100,000 between the house and the street, with draw calls rising too), add roughly 1.5 ms, landing near 4.5 ms mean with the 99th percentile still well under 8; that keeps the whole of Kohar's enemy load (about 3 ms at 12 alive in B12) affordable on top, within 11. Not measured: Safari's graphics-card time (it cannot be read), other machines, a larger window, a whole map. |

| E33 — Step A: second map with height (2026-09-29; report only, nothing built, downloaded or deployed) | **Request (user):** a second, multi-level map (interiors, stairs, ladders, usable roofs, fall damage for players and enemies, bodies that fall and leave blood), Kohar Valley untouched; Ambush arena with a rooftop start wanted. **Facts read from the code:** `player.y = groundY()` every frame and jump is a camera offset (`jumpY`); `blocked`/`move`/`segClear` test X/Z footprints of `solids` with no vertical extent; navigation is one 91×91 grid of 2 m cells; six places set an actor's height from `groundY`; map bounds, the grid, spawns and mission positions are constants in `game.js` and the world is built once at load (there is no map object). Already three-dimensional: `visible()`, `clear()`, `hitScan`, blast and smoke tests are real rays against meshes, the blood decal pool exists, and `pose` and `snapshot` already carry x, y, z. No model loader is bundled (only `three.module.js`, `BufferGeometryUtils`, `RGBELoader`; the rifle is a JSON file). **Requires:** map as data (Kohar as map 1, trace-identical); standing surfaces with a floor lookup, solids with vertical extent, stairs as ramps, ladders as climb volumes, real vertical velocity, fall damage; layered navigation with links (stair, ladder, drop) and per-level cover; throwables landing on floors; co-op vertical state, rewind and ranges in 3D; a levels view on the maps. **Recommendation:** build the height system on a small grey-box yard of its own with Kohar running the same code in the flat case and proven identical by the existing traces; do not add ladders to Kohar. Order: map as data; player height; enemy height; co-op height; look slice (loader, CC0 materials and props, user approves the look, Safari measured); layout in grey-box as Skirmish; dressing; Ambush rooftop arena; Story operation last. About 30 to 45 dev-days without Story. **Assets:** Poly Haven, ambientCG, Kenney and Quaternius licence pages read 2026-09-29: all CC0, redistribution allowed, no attribution required. Kenney and Quaternius are stylised low-poly and do not match the look. No free source offers a coherent set of realistic multi-storey buildings: buildings stay made by code, from a modular kit, dressed with CC0 materials and props. **Correction:** what is filed under M5.03/M5.04 is Microsoft Rocketbox (MIT) and Mixamo (Adobe terms) for characters and hands, not CC0 and not map assets, and nothing was ever downloaded. **Frame budget:** today 3.2–3.7 ms mean, p99 6–8 ms, 320–360 draw calls, 160–167 thousand triangles (B12, B16). Proposed ceilings for the new map, to be measured: 500 draw calls, 350 thousand triangles in view, 1k textures, no dynamic lights, static shadows, batching by block so that frustum culling works. GPU time cannot be read in Safari. **Unverified:** every estimate; individual asset availability; visual quality. |
| E34 — Map project: the user's decisions (2026-09-29) | **Build order:** the nine builds of E33, with the look slice moved to second, immediately after map as data: (1) Kohar Valley as map data, **done, Build 21**; (2) look slice; (3) player height; (4) enemy height; (5) co-op height; (6) layout; (7) dressing; (8) Ambush arena with a rooftop start; (9) Story operation. Reason (user): "I want to see and approve the visual direction before committing to fifteen more builds of system work." **Setting:** a hill town built in terraces; working name **Dehrun Terraces** (given by Claude at the user's request, fictional, the user may rename it). **Mantling: yes**, by explicit request; the standing rule against stairs and mantling is lifted for the map project (M1.04). Reason (user): without it every parapet needs a gap, and roofs must be fallen off. **Fall damage:** none up to 3 m, rising to fatal at 12 m, the same for enemies. **Assets:** CC0 preferred; assets that require a credit line are also allowed (the game has a credits page); no paid assets. Nothing of this is built: Build 21 only files it. |
| E35 — The nine builds in real time (2026-09-29, asked by the user) | The estimates in E33 are developer-days, the roadmap's unit. **Measured on this build:** estimated 2 to 3 developer-days; done in one working session of about three hours, of which about forty minutes were lost to the machine sleeping during a test run and about an hour was test runs and the breakage pass. Builds 16 to 20 were the same size in the same unit and took one to two hours each by the user's account. **So one developer-day of estimate has been about one hour of a session.** In those terms: look slice 3 to 4 h; player height 4 to 5 h; enemy height 5 to 8 h, likely two sessions; co-op height 3 to 4 h; layout 4 to 6 h plus 2 to 3 h for changing map inside a running page (MAP-01), likely two sessions; dressing 4 to 6 h, likely two sessions; Ambush arena 4 to 5 h; Story operation 5 to 8 h. **About 30 to 40 hours of sessions without Story, in 10 to 13 sessions.** What this does not count, and what will decide the calendar: the user's playtests between builds; rounds of visual correction, which need the user's eyes (each round about an hour); choosing and approving assets before they are downloaded. **Unverified:** every figure is an estimate from one measured build and five reported ones. |
| E36 — Look slice: the approved assets (2026-09-29, Build 22) | The list was given to the user before anything was downloaded and approved as it stood, with the sky, the 30 MB cap kept, and no cut of prop pictures to 256 px or of the surface set. **Source: Poly Haven, all CC0**; the loader is Three.js r169 `GLTFLoader.js`, MIT. **Downloaded (raw): 80.3 MB in 198 files. Bundled after processing: 28.7 MB** (surfaces 18.3 MB, models 9.3 MB, sky 1.1 MB, loader 0.1 MB). Processing: surface pictures kept at 1,024 px and saved again as JPEG at quality 88; each model's pictures made 512 px at quality 82 and the model packed with them into one `.glb`. **Nothing was cut to stay under the cap**; at quality 88 for the models too the total was 30.05 MB, so the models are at 82 and the surfaces, which are seen from closest, at 88. **Surfaces (15):** plastered_wall_03, rough_plaster_broken, white_rough_plaster, yellow_stone_wall, rustic_stone_wall_02, cobblestone_floor_04, monastery_stone_floor, rocky_trail, sandy_gravel_02, clay_roof_tiles_02, rusty_corrugated_iron, weathered_brown_planks, blue_painted_planks, brown_floor_tiles, rusty_metal_shutter. **Models (28), triangles measured:** barrel_03 1,473; brass_pot_02 3,344; cement_bag 844; ceramic_pot 3,592; ceramic_vase_04 9,000 (listed as 2,224 polygons); industrial_wall_lamp 4,446; metal_jug 6,040; moon_rock_02 3,304; old_tyre 2,880; painted_wooden_bench 630, _cabinet 2,227, _chair_01 724, _shelves 524, _stool 676, _table 600; planter_pot_clay 3,080; potted_plant_04 8,929 (listed 6,097); propane_tank 5,238; vintage_day_bed 2,715; vintage_oil_lamp 7,208; wine_barrel_01 10,820; wooden_bowl_02 4,666; wooden_bucket_01 5,116; wooden_crate_01 6,576; wooden_crate_02 5,176; wooden_ladder_02 6,126; wooden_lantern_01 8,321; wooden_table_02 196. **Sky:** syferfontein_18d_clear_puresky, 1k. Authors are named on the credits page. **Consequence for the props:** the first placing (86 props) came to 344,000 triangles for the props alone, over the ceiling with the houses; 15 of the heaviest were taken out or exchanged for light ones (71 props, 250,000). The free models are heavy for their size; a map of many blocks needs lighter copies of the heaviest (MAP-03). |
| E37 — Critique of the look slice (2026-09-29, Build 23; the block walked in the desktop app's browser, Safari not used) | **The user's two findings.** (1) Flicker: confirmed and wider than seen. 442 exposed places where two different surfaces lay in one plane; the largest 151 m² on each side of the street (the upper terrace's ground ending in the face of the retaining wall: the orange ground through the stone, as the user saw); the rest in the kit: roofs level with wall tops, lintels with the wall they carry, sills, thresholds, footings, the dark panel behind an opening level with its frame, wall corners. **Fixed.** (2) The view that looked off: all five readings agreed with. Awning a flat plane in a colour of its own: **fixed**. Openings black voids: **fixed**. Beam ends evenly spaced: **fixed**. Overhang unsupported (the balconies): **fixed**. Bare terrain meeting the block abruptly: **fixed in part** (houses and field walls beyond; the hill itself is still plain). **Found besides.** The sun in the sky picture stood south-east while the light came from the west: **fixed** (the sky is turned, `sky.turn`). Shade nearly black: **fixed** (ambient 1.85). Long walls repeating every 2.6 m and no dirt at the foot of any wall: **fixed** (shade in the geometry's colours). Yards like orange carpet, a strip of bare earth along every front: **fixed** (trodden ground, paving). White boulders (a moon rock): **fixed** (tint, sunk a little). A post in the workshop doorway: **fixed**. Lanterns hung from nothing, one in the ceiling: **fixed** (cords, lowered). A wall lamp between beam ends at second-floor height: **fixed** (beside the door). **Left:** the boundary walls are large blank planes; the hill is one plain ground; interiors have one lamp; no plants; see MAP-03. **What will cause trouble once it is played:** bullets pass through small props, shutters, door leaves and glass; a grenade that misses what is built falls below the street; everything is drawn whether in view or not; **enemies cannot use the doors** (1.05 m against navigation on 2 m squares with .9 m clearance); anything solid is solid to any height; figures indoors will be lit as if outdoors; the kit makes about 3,300 boxes, many of them small occluders that every sight check tests one by one; the texture memory is unmeasured; the static shadow map gets coarser as the map grows. |
| E38 — Layout requirement: the Ambush arena of Dehrun Terraces (user, 2026-09-29; for the layout build) | **User concern:** the look slice is very dense and no building in it is large enough for an Ambush arena. **Requirement:** Dehrun Terraces must be laid out with **a large multi-storey building designed as the Ambush arena from the start**: internal stairs, a roof, and a street around it, so that a run can start upstairs and buy its way down and out. **It must not be carved out of a layout designed for something else**, which is what made the Kohar Valley arena cramped. **If the layout build concludes that this cannot work within the map, it reports that rather than compromising**, and the user will consider a dedicated Ambush map instead. Consequences already known (E37): doors must be wide enough for enemies (1.05 m doors cannot be used by navigation on 2 m squares with .9 m clearance), and the triangle ceiling leaves no room in a block furnished as the look slice is (MAP-03). |
| E39 — Falling somewhere with no way out (2026-09-30, Build 24, asked by the user) | **The block today has no such place**, checked by walking, pulling up and dropping from 400 places in eight directions (T34 `bounds`) and by hand: every roof a body can reach has a way down (over its parapet and a drop of 4.6 to 6.5 m, which hurts but does not kill: A's roof costs about 24 health; C's roof, reached by its outside stair, about 19); the terraces join by the steps; the yards are open to the street; the hatch drops into a room with a stair; a body cannot get outside the boundary walls (3.1 m against a 1.35 m pull-up), and **the map's edges now hold at every height** (found while writing this: from the low house's roof by the south gate a running jump could have reached the boundary wall's coping and dropped outside, where the walked ground and the drawn hill part company and nothing leads back; the edge rule of the flat game now applies in the space too, T34 checks that jump). The tall house E's roof (11.8 m) cannot be reached, so its 9 m drop is not on offer. **On a whole map it will happen** unless it is prevented: a light well, a sunken yard, a gap between two houses, a roof with parapets higher than 1.35 m all round. **Recommendation:** (1) **a check at build time, in the map's suite**: flood over every surface a body can stand on, with the body's own rules (step, pull-up, drop), from the start; every surface reached must have a way back to a street that a body survives; the suite fails on any island, naming it; this is the same kind of check as T34's `bounds`, made complete, and belongs to the layout build (each block is checked as it is laid out); (2) **a last resort in the game**, cheap: a body that has not been able to move for some seconds, or stands on a surface the check marked as an island, is offered E · CLIMB BACK, which puts it on the last street it stood on; in co-op, a fall that kills is already a death the teammate can revive from; (3) never gaps or pits by design without a ladder or steps. Nothing of this is built. |
| E40 — The kit and the Ambush arena (2026-09-30, Build 24, asked by the user: can the kit build the arena of E38?) | **Plainly: not as it stands.** **The largest building it can make today:** the tall house E, 9 × 12 m and three storeys (8.6 m to the roof), and the open house A: 8.8 × 11 m, two storeys, one room a floor. A house is four outer walls a storey with openings, a floor slab a storey (with rectangular wells since Build 24), one straight stair flight along a wall, a flat roof behind a parapet, a ladder and hatch to the roof. There are **no interior partitions, no rooms within a floor, no doors between rooms, no corridors, no stair with a landing or a turn, no railing at a well, no roof door**; every storey of a house is one open room or nothing. The arena of E38 (say 26 × 18 m, three storeys and a roof, several rooms and a corridor a floor, two stairs, doors onto the street on more than one side, a stair head onto the roof) needs, in the kit: (1) **rooms**: interior wall segments per storey with door openings (the `wall` function already makes any wall with openings; what is missing is a `rooms` description per storey and the lining on both faces); (2) **stairs that turn**: flights with landings and a second run, and railings at the well (the `flight` function makes one straight run); (3) **a roof door** from a stair head (a small box on the roof with a door) instead of a hatch; (4) **doors wide enough for enemies** (1.05 m today; enemy navigation on 2 m squares with .9 m clearance cannot pass them: E37) or finer navigation, to be decided in the enemy-height build; (5) **more than one storey lined and furnished** (only the ground floor and, since Build 24, one upper room are); (6) lamps per room (glow only). Not needed: new assets or a change in how it is drawn (the batching draws each surface in one call whatever the size). **Estimate: 4 to 6 hours as its own build, "kit interiors", building the arena itself as the proof, in this block or beside it.** **Recommendation: do it before the layout build** (as map build 3.5, between player height and enemy height, or straight after enemy height): the layout build must place the arena first, and cannot until the kit can describe one; and the enemy-height build wants real interiors to navigate. **If the user would rather change course:** a dedicated Ambush map made of the same kit would need the same extension, so the extension is needed either way; what a separate map saves is fitting the arena into a town. |
| E41 — Door widths for enemies (2026-09-30, Build 25, asked by the user) | **Chosen: 1.3 m for every door and doorway of the customs house** (street doors, doors between rooms, roof doors; the stair openings are 2.7 m, each flight 1.37 m). An enemy in the game asks .45 m either side of its line (`NAV_R`); a door's frame posts stand .08 m proud on each side, so 1.3 m leaves 1.14 m between them, .24 m more than needed. **Found, not fixed: the block's own doors (1.05 m, Build 22) leave .89 m between their posts — 1 cm less than a .45 m body needs in the space** (in the flat 2D navigation of today the posts are not solids and the opening is 1.05 m, so Kohar-style enemies would pass). Changing them is one number in `dist/map-dehrun.js` (`door()`), a deliberate change to the block; left for the user's word, since this build was to leave the block as it was. **Also found:** under the player's stepping rule a body of radius .45 cannot begin a stair of .35 m treads (its front reaches the second tread, .42 m up, before its middle is on the first); enemies (map build 5) need a stair rule of their own (judge the step under the body's middle), which is the right answer; making every tread .46 m long is the alternative. |
| E42 — Enemy height: what the geometry gives (2026-09-30, Build 26) | **Sight between levels is what the parapets and sills allow** (user decision: keep them). A .6 m parapet hides the square from the roof, and the roof from the square, within about 9 m of the wall; a .8 m window sill hides the street within about 12 m of a first-floor window. Cross-level fights therefore happen through windows at a distance and in the stairwells; an enemy at a window holds and fires where a room gives no cover with a firing line. **Cover per level exists** (the table has points on every floor) and is taken where a firing line exists (the first-floor bout took cover). **Stairs:** the enemies' rule (treads within .65 m above the feet do not stand in the wide body's way) climbs every stair of the map; house A's inside stair had to be widened to 1.05 m for the body itself (E41). **Drops:** the navigation walks off edges no higher than 3 m (house C's open stair side, house A's stair well and roof hatch); higher edges are never planned, only fallen from when pushed or shoved. **Not done, by design or scope:** enemies do not pull themselves up (no mantling), do not crouch under low things, and the teammate and co-op know nothing of height (map build 6). |
| E43 — Co-op height: what was flat (2026-09-30, Build 27) | Positions always travelled as three numbers (poses `p`, snapshots `host` and `actors`), `rewind` kept y, `friendlyHit` and the knife and blast rays were three-dimensional, grenades raycast the kit's floors. What was flat: **(1)** the guest's health is the host's to keep, and the host never knew of a guest's fall — now `remoteHeight` charges it from the poses (a jump, a mantle and a ladder are told apart: the ladder by the pose's `cl`); the guest's own `land()` charges nothing while guesting (else twice). **(2)** revive reach was measured across only: 3.35 m of floor counted as 0 — now three-dimensional on maps with height, on both pages and on the host's acceptance of a guest's `revive` (with half a metre of slack for the way there). **(3)** `mateNow()` put the extrapolated teammate on the terrain: a grenade on the ground floor under a teammate on the first floor found it at its feet. **(4)** a look map never ran the AI or the guest's drawing when connected, and hid the teammate as one of the map's actors. **(5)** crouching under things (`mustCrouch`) was not sent. **Decision kept:** the guest's fall is judged by the host from 12.5 poses a second: the peak is caught within 3 cm, the landing exactly (the floor's height is known), so the charge is within a point of the guest's own reckoning. |
| E44 — The customs house as the Ambush arena in the town (2026-09-30, Build 28; the user asked whether its position works for a run that starts upstairs and buys its way down and out) | **It works, and the square is what makes it work.** The customs house stands on the square (x −13..13, z 53..71), which is walled on three sides and opens by four gateways: north to the block's street (the gate at z 44), west and east to the lower districts (the square's gates at z 62), and south to the lower town (the gate at z 80, 1.6 m down the steps). A run that starts on the second floor or the roof (the loft is 143 m², the roof has two stair heads and a parapet) has the two stairs as its first two gates, the ground floor's four street doors as the next four, and the square's four gateways as the last ring: seven or ten purchasable openings in a natural order, each one letting the waves in from a new direction (the enemies already climb both stairs both ways, pass every door and take cover on every level; the reinforcement entries of Skirmish are those same gateways). "Out" is real: through any of the square's gateways lies a district or the lower town, and the extraction can stand at the south gate (the map's extraction point, [0, 100]) or on the block's street. What the Ambush build must decide: (1) whether the barricades are the customs house's doors and stair heads (inside-out) or the square's gateways (outside-in) first; (2) where the crates go (the roof, the loft and the square's corners are the natural places); (3) the out-of-bounds line (the square's walls, with the districts opened area by area). What it must not do: put spawns inside the square while any gateway is shut (the flood check would be the guard). **Nothing about the arena is built in Build 28**; Ambush stays off the new map. |
| E45 — Step A: why the stairs felt tight at 1.7 m (2026-09-30, Build 29; measured before anything was built) | **Asked:** the actual clear width on each flight, the bodies' widths, what two bodies passing needs, against real stairs (domestic about .9 m, public 1.2 m or more); whether the trouble is the flight width, the railings, the headroom, the landings or the way the body slides along walls; a number. **Measured (all 23 flights, a probe from each side at body height, halfway up):** the customs house's flights 1.66 m clear on the partition side and 1.76 on the wall side (made 1.77), the block's and the districts' 1.69 to 1.70. **Bodies:** the player is a square .68 m across, an enemy .90 (a person's shoulders are about .5); a player and an enemy side by side need 1.58 m, two players 1.36, two enemies 1.80. Bodies do not collide with each other, so "passing" never stopped anyone: what stops a body is walls. **So the width was already more than a public stair**, and wide enough for a player and an enemy with 8 to 19 cm to spare (not for two enemies). **Headroom** on the centre line 2.12 to 2.58 m against a 1.8 m body: not the cause. **Railings:** posts 4 cm thick every .35 m, which a body slides along as along a wall: not the cause. **What was the cause: (1) the way the body slides along walls.** The mover slid along a plain wall but stopped dead at anything standing proud of it: walking every flight pressed diagonally into each wall, the body was stopped **21 times on 17 of the 23 flights** — by beam ends 3 to 6 cm proud at head height in the customs house's west stair (every storey), the stub of the partition at the east stair's entry, the posts of the roof doors, rafters over house A's stair, and the copings and string courses of the houses beside every district stair. A player going up a stair at the wall was halted and had to steer away: that is "tight". **(2) The landings:** the half landings were 1.5 m deep as made, 1.37 m clear, with .69 m of play for a .68 m body turning right round, against .98 to 1.08 m of play on the flights they join. **(3) The roof doors** at the top of each stair were 1.3 m (about 1.1 clear) on a 1.77 m flight. **(4) The camera** is at the middle of the body: on a 1.66 m flight it has .98 m of play from side to side; a person on a real 1.2 m stair has about .7, so the stair was already generous by that measure too, but the snags made the usable part the middle third. **Recommended and built:** flights **2.0 m clear** (1.32 m of play; a player and an enemy with .42 m to spare, two enemies with .2), half landings as deep as a flight is wide (2.0 made, 1.87 clear), roof doors 1.9 m (1.69 clear), and **the player's moves nudged past anything up to .2 m proud of a wall** (`BODY.nudge`): with that, the same walk along every wall is stopped nowhere. The nudge, not the width, is what removes the tightness; the width was asked for and is the smaller part. Enemies are not nudged (their navigation keeps them off walls). |
| E46 — Step A: what makes a stair feel cramped when its width is not the cause (2026-09-30, Build 30; measured before anything was built) | **The user has called the stairs tight at 1.37, 1.7 and 2.0 m; each time the width measured wide enough.** Measured on the customs house's west stair (treads .35 by .2125 m, flights 2.12 m, half landing 1.87 by 4.07 m clear) with the game's own keys, frame by frame. **(1) How the body is held to the treads, and the eye with it: THE CAUSE.** The body is put on each tread as its middle crosses the riser, and the eye was the feet plus 1.7 m with nothing between: **the eye rose 21 cm in a single frame, 10.2 times a second walking and 16.4 sprinting**, and stood still between. A wall 1 m from the eye jumps 12° of the 70° view each time, a wall at the body's edge (.34 m) 32°, the soffit .4 to .9 m overhead more. On the flat and on the lanes' steps (which the map's height makes ramps) the eye never jumps; only in the kit's stairs, which are the closest spaces in the game. A close space that strobes reads as a cramped one. Going down the same; the body never leaves the treads (no frame off the ground, no landing bob). **(2) Headroom over the eye line:** 2.12 to 2.58 m over the treads on the centre line, so .42 to .88 m over the 1.7 m eye. A real stair (2.0 to 2.1 m headroom, a 1.6 m eye) gives .4 to .5. Not the cause. **(3) The walls at eye height:** in the middle of a flight the eye is 1.0 to 1.1 m from each side; nothing stands nearer at eye height than at the feet but the beam ends Build 29's nudge passes. Not the cause. **(4) The turn at the half landing:** 1.87 m deep and 4.07 across; a body .68 m square turns in it freely. But a flight takes .78 s walking and .37 s sprinting (the body climbs at the flat's speed, 3.43 m/s along and 2.1 m/s up: four to seven times a person's pace), so a full turn comes every .8 s: hurried, which may read as tight. A contributor, not measured as a fault. **(5) Field of view:** 70° top to bottom (about 96° across at 16:10), 76° sprinting: the same on a stair as anywhere. Not the cause. **(6) The camera's height on a slope:** the eye is 1.7 m over the tread under the body's middle; looking level up a flight the treads meet the eye line 2.8 m ahead and the flight above is .36 to .75 m from the eye measured square to the slope. That is what a dog-leg stair is; a real one is the same. Not the cause. **(7) The railings:** posts 4 cm thick and .92 m high under a handrail about 1 m up, on the well's centre line between the flights: .7 m below the eye, not at its height. Not the cause. **Recommended and built:** ease the eye over the treads (`eyeStep`): held back by each step the feet take, given back at the feet's own climbing rate. Measured after: no frame moves the eye more than 4.2 cm walking or 7.6 sprinting, up or down; it is never more than .18 m behind the feet and back over them .34 s after stopping. **Recommended, not built (the user's decision, it touches class speeds):** slow the body on treads to about .7 of its speed. **Not recommended:** widening again. |
| E47 — The stairs at 2.4 m and what the layout paid (2026-10-01, Build 31; the user: "if widening further would break the layout, report that rather than compromising quietly") | **Proposed and built: 2.4 m clear.** A player and an enemy pass with .8 m to spare, two enemies with .6, three players abreast. **The customs house** (26 by 18 m, a 3 m corridor through its middle): a dog-leg stair of two 2.4 m flights needs a well 5.1 m wide; with eight .36 m treads and a landing no shallower than a flight is wide it needs 5.3 m of length, and the house has 6.4 m north of the corridor and 7.8 m south of it. A 1.1 m closet at the end of the west stair could have no door. So **the wells take the whole depth: the six closets (two a storey) are gone**, with their six doors, and the twelve windows in the closets' outer walls are gone too (a window at storey height would be cut by the half landing; the landings have no windows until dressing gives them their own). The north-west rooms are 5.5 and 4.5 m wide (were 6.3 and 5.3), the south-east rooms 3.5 m (were 4.3; 27 m², the smallest room). The half landings came out 3.45 and 4.85 m deep, the entries 4.84 m, the roof doors 2.19 m clear. **House A** (8.8 m wide): its well is 2.46 m, leaving 5.9 m of room beside it. **The district houses:** the outside stair and its 2.4 m landing take the whole south face; on a 12 m house 25 treads of .232 m, on a 10 m house 20 treads of .29 m (it was .276; a tread may not be shorter than .355 m for the player's body, so the rise is what gives). **Wider than 2.4 m:** the customs house would lose another .5 m of its corner rooms per .25 m of flight (2.9 m flights would leave the south-east rooms 2.5 m wide), and a 10 m district house's stair would pass .33 m a tread, the kit's limit: a bigger customs house (30 m) or 12 m district houses would be needed. **Recommendation: stop at 2.4.** |

| H2 — GitHub repository and Pages (2026-09-27) | Repository https://github.com/alex-wilson141/Dustline, public, branch `main`. Live game https://alex-wilson141.github.io/Dustline/ (Pages build type: workflow, HTTPS enforced). **Publishing process:** commit on `main` and `git push`. GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/pages.yml`) then checks that the build stamp is current (`node tools/stamp-build.mjs --check`), runs every `tests/test-*.mjs` suite on Node 24, and only if all pass publishes `dist/` as the site root to GitHub Pages. A failing check publishes nothing and the previous build stays live. Players get the new build on their next page load: every module and asset URL carries a content hash and the page checks its build token against the server at start-up (DEPLOY-01), so nobody runs a mix of old and new files. Both co-op players must be on the same build: reload once after a release. **Remote before the push:** one commit `bcc2578` "Add files via upload" (2026-09-23), 42 files (33 `dist`, 5 `tests`, `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `DUSTLINE-ROADMAP.md`, `package.json`), menu label BUILD 04, identical to the local Build 04 baseline except for the missing `.gitignore` and `.claude/launch.json` and one roadmap line; no tags, releases, issues, pull requests, Pages or collaborators. Replaced with `git push --force-with-lease=main:bcc2578`. **Privacy check of everything pushed (all commits):** no tokens, keys, passwords or `.env` files; author and committer of all commits are `320872799+alex-wilson141@users.noreply.github.com`; the macOS username no longer appears in any commit. Still in the public text by design: the address of the earlier public Site and its hosting project ID (identifiers, not credentials). The replaced upload commit `bcc2578` is no longer in the history, but GitHub may keep a detached copy reachable by its full ID for a while; its roadmap contains the old file path. **History rewrite, old → new commit IDs:** Build 14 `96041f5` → `9d41cd8`; Build 13 `0315f85` → `1840e3b`; Build 12 `55d81c7` → `a43641f`; Build 11 `2433346` → `ebc5b36`; Build 10 `2394d6f` → `803aec3`; Build 09 `21abfb6` → `6560f7a`; Build 08 cleanup `5722dea` → `2b295f1`; Build 08 `bc6b11b` → `600a65a`; Build 07 `899ee2f` → `e4b36bd`; Build 06 `de92234` → `ce1d05c`; Build 05 `c80076f` → `5a07664`; F3 overlay `1d21a46` → `087b2e8`; Build 04 baseline `4f8e1ee` → `611048b`. Older rows of the progress log cite the old IDs. Four suites fetch earlier builds with `git show` and now name the new IDs (T13, T14, T16, T17); the Build 09 trace fixture is identified by the SHA-256 of its source, which is unchanged. All 17 suites pass in a fresh clone (167 checks). A bundle of the original history is kept outside the repository on the user's Mac. **Co-op:** unchanged code; the page is served over HTTPS, which WebRTC needs; connection setup uses Google STUN and there is no TURN relay (NET-01), so two players behind restrictive networks may not connect. |
| T25 — Co-op handshake (`tests/test-coop-handshake.mjs`, 2026-09-28, Build 15) | 6 checks with the real `PeerSquad` and the real game buttons over a stand-in for the browser's `RTCPeerConnection`: (1) a code is `DUSTLINE:<H or A>:<length>:<payload>:<checksum>`, uses only letters, digits, `:`, `-` and `_`, and reads back to the same description; (2) a valid exchange connects and messages flow both ways, untouched and through eleven manglings (wrapping at 76 and 64 columns with Unix and Windows line breaks, spaces, tabs, non-breaking and zero-width spaces, straight and curly quotes, backticks, angle brackets, lower-case header, words in front); (3) routing by what the code is: the host pressing JOIN WITH CODE with the answer connects; a host code on ACCEPT ANSWER, an answer on a page with no host code, an answer made for an earlier host code and your own code each get their own message and leave a pending host code intact; (4) wrong input: empty, web address, random text, cut short at four lengths with the counts, one character changed, an inner change that only the checksum can notice, a character removed or added, two codes in one paste, a Build 14 code, a newer build: each rejected with its own message, none with the old generic line, none changing the connection state; (5) network failure: after a correct exchange a failed connection, or none within the time limit, says the networks could not reach each other and names the side without a public address; (6) the buttons: the box is empty while a new code is prepared, COPY CODE says what it copied and how long it is, the paste box is cleared after a code is taken and kept after a rejection. **Breakage pass** on a scratch copy: 23 mutations; first run 21 caught, the checksum mutation survived (the changed character was also detectable otherwise; a checksum-only case was added: caught) and one mutation had not applied (its pattern did not match the source; corrected: caught). **Final 23/23.** **Real browser:** two tabs on the local server exchanged a host code with 19 line breaks and an answer with spaces, the host pressed JOIN WITH CODE with the answer, and both connected through a real WebRTC channel; empty, link, cut-short and own-code pastes showed their messages. **Not verified:** two players on two networks (needs the user and a friend; nothing is pushed yet). |

### Running and reproducing checks

From the project root: `python3 -m http.server 8765 --directory dist`, then visit `http://localhost:8765/` in a browser supporting WebGL and pointer lock. Serve over HTTP; opening HTML as a file cannot load the modules. Deployment is Sites via `.openai/hosting.json`, project ID `appgprj_6ab34f9cda948191942d1ea5af14bb9f`; preserve its **public** audience unless asked otherwise. Record publication separately from local implementation and code verification.

M1 regression scripts now live in this project: run `node tests/test-sprint-m1.mjs` and `node tests/test-framefire-m1.mjs` from the project root. Both use `tests/sprint-harness.mjs` to execute the production game module with browser/rendering/network substitutes. Historical pre-M1 measured results are preserved in `tests/fixtures/sprint-before-m1.json`; they are evidence from base `07a6e8f`, not generated from current code. Older checks and the baseline reproduction script remain in the surrounding workspace at `../../work/`; from that workspace, run `node work/<script>.mjs`. Node used here: `~/.cache/codex-runtimes/codex-primary-runtime/dependencies/node/bin/node`.

Build 14: `node tests/test-ambush-b14.mjs` (T24). Build 13: `node tests/test-ambush-b13.mjs` (T23, rewritten in Build 14). Build 12: `node tests/test-ambush-b12.mjs` (T22). Build 11: `node tests/test-ambush-b11.mjs` (T21). Build 10: `node tests/test-ambush-b10.mjs` (T20); `node tests/test-ambush-b10.mjs --record <path/to/game.js> "<label>"` re-records its Story/Skirmish baseline `tests/fixtures/story-skirmish-ai-b09.json` from a given source (use the Build 09 source `git show 6560f7a:dist/game.js`), for example after a JS-engine change alters floating point in the last bits.

T1/T3–T5 record the setup checks on Build 03. T2 and T6 were rerun after the final M1 fire gate; T7–T9 record the M1 diagnosis and Build 04 regressions. Later reruns must record their own results rather than treating a past pass as current acceptance.

| DEPLOY-01 | DONE (code, Build 08 cleanup, 2026-09-24); verified in Safari 18.5 and Chromium (B4) | Browsers could run a new `game.js` with older cached modules (E20). **Chosen: versioned URLs, not no-cache headers.** Headers depend on the host, and none of ours lets us rely on them. `python3 -m http.server` cannot set them: it sends Last-Modified only, which is what triggers the heuristic caching. The public Site host exposes no per-file headers, and GitHub Pages sets its own. `<meta>` cache tags don't apply to modules. Versioned URLs travel with the files and need no server configuration; browsers key their cache and module map on the full URL including the query. **Implementation:**
  - **Token:** `tools/stamp-build.mjs` computes a 12-hex token from every `.js`/`.css` file, the generated `build.js` and `index.html`.
  - **Page:** `index.html` carries the token in a `dustline-build` meta tag, the stylesheet URL and an import map that gives every module a `?v=<token>` URL. Source import lines stay plain, so Node tests keep one module instance.
  - **Assets:** `dist/build.js` holds a content hash per asset, and code loads assets through `assetURL()`.
  - **Start-up check:** `index.html` fetches the served page with `cache:'reload'`, bypassing the cache and replacing a stale cached copy. It starts `game.js?v=<token>` only when the tokens match. A different token loads the server's page under `?build=<token>`, and an error or unstamped answer loads it once under `?build=none-<t>`. It never loops: after one retry it shows a reload message. If the server is unreachable, this copy starts.
  - **Upgrade guard:** `build.js`, imported by every module changed since Build 08, replaces an old unstamped page before any game module runs.
  - **Maintenance:** run `node tools/stamp-build.mjs` after every change in `dist/`; T17 fails until you do.

  **Limits:**
  - A static server ignores `?v=`: the version is a browser-cache key, not something the server enforces. Changing `dist/` while a page is loading (a `git pull` or `checkout`, or a non-atomic upload) can still pair new names with old bytes for that load. Reload after updating and deploy atomically.
  - A CDN that drops query strings from its cache key (e.g. CloudFront CachingOptimized, Cloudflare's "Ignore Query String") would defeat the scheme; python http.server and GitHub Pages key on the full URL.
  - Before Safari 16.4 there are no import maps: modules load unversioned, though the start-up check still runs.
  - Once, after updating from Build 08, a still-cached Build 08 page can keep running Build 08 whole until an ordinary reload. |
| AI-04 | DONE (code, Build 08 cleanup, 2026-09-24); human feel UNVERIFIED | User playtest: "an enemy actively fighting me turned away mid-fight and went back to patrolling." Three causes (E21):
  - **Perception:** enemies checked only the three nearest possible targets for sight.
  - **Engagement lifetime:** a 6 s sight timer ended the engagement while the target was alive and near, and the search ended in patrol.
  - **Flapping:** heard shots re-armed the threat without restarting that timer, so the enemy flipped state every frame.

  **Fix** (`game.js` enemy sensing and `enemyPlan`, `foeLive`; `engageLeash` 60 m in `enemy-ai.js`):
  - Enemies check every possible target in sight range and look for their current foe first.
  - An enemy remembers its foe, the target it last saw. While that foe is alive and within `engageLeash`, its threat is kept and losing sight starts a hunt toward the last known position, which ends back in combat (hold, cover) rather than duty.
  - (Re)engaging restarts the sight timer.
  - A dead or distant foe returns the enemy to duty as before.

  **Unchanged:** fire cadence, hit chance and damage (fire block and `aiHit` byte-identical), the three-shooter limit, and the relay rule. During a relay capture an enemy inside the 16 m ring still walks out without firing first, then re-engages. T18. |

| PVP-01 | DONE (code, Build 18); real connections UNVERIFIED | **Fixed:** every shot is judged against what its shooter was looking at. The guest draws each character on the straight line between the last two snapshots, at the moment one snapshot interval (85 ms) before its clock; its shot names that moment and its firing position; the host puts every enemy and its own player back where they were then, judges, and restores. **Cap: 300 ms** from the picture to the judging (the delay there and back plus the 85 ms), which covers about 105 ms each way; an older claim is judged at 300 ms, a future or broken one now; a firing position more than 1.7 m from where the host has the guest is replaced by the host's. The host's own shots are judged in the present, which is what the host sees. **Friendly fire:** the damage rule and body were already equal; the timing now is too (T28 checks 2 and 3). **1v1 deathmatch (F.15):** this removes the blocker as it was filed. What a 1v1 still needs before it is fair is listed under F.15. Not verified: a real connection with changing delay and loss. Earlier text: | **Player-against-player shots are decided by the host, so timing favours the host.** The damage rule and the body shape are the same both ways since Build 16 (T26 check 9), but the host fires at what it sees, while the guest fires at a picture that is one snapshot (up to 80 ms) plus the connection delay old and is judged against where the host is now; and the host's view of the guest is up to one pose interval (80 ms) old. In co-op this only matters for friendly fire. **Blocker for F.14 and F.15.** Needs: shots judged against where the target was when the shooter fired (rewind by the shooter's delay), or both pages confirming a hit. |

| COOP-01 | DONE (Build 15); **confirmed by the user's play on two networks, 2026-09-28** | **Report:** "Wrong connection code. Use the matching offer or answer from DUSTLINE." on every attempt between two networks. **Where:** one line of `decode()` in `network.js`, reached only after the pasted text had decoded cleanly, and before any network activity. **Conditions for that message:** format version not 2 (every build since Build 04 writes 2), the wrong kind of code, no description, or a description not starting `v=0`; codes made by the game can only meet the second. **How the buttons led there (both reproduced on Build 14 in a browser):** (a) the host pastes the answer and presses JOIN WITH CODE instead of ACCEPT ANSWER; (b) after an earlier attempt the old answer stays in the code box while CREATE HOST CODE prepares the new code, and COPY CODE pressed at once copies the stale answer. **Transit:** a Build 14 code is about 1,420 characters of letters, digits, `+`, `/` and `=`; line breaks and spaces were tolerated; truncation, quotes or a pasted link gave a different message, so the users' codes were not mangled. **Live versus local:** `network.js`, `game.js` and `index.html` were byte-identical; fingerprinting cannot cause it. **Verdict:** code handling; the attempts never reached the network. **Fix:** codes `DUSTLINE:<H or A>:<length>:<payload>:<checksum>` in a chat-safe alphabet; `readCode()` with reasons empty, link, not-a-code, incomplete (with counts), altered, old-build, newer-build; `PeerSquad.submit()` routes by kind; answers name the host code they were made for; the code box is emptied while preparing; a failed or timed-out connection after a correct exchange says so (NET-01). The handshake format changed, so both players need Build 15. The earlier two-tab verification (H3) could not have caught this: it used the right buttons in the right order on one network. |

Fix, Ambush planner only: a stuck watchdog (`stallTime` 3 s without `stallStep` 1 m of movement while on the move with route ahead) steps the enemy to the nearest navigable spot (usually under half a metre), takes another slot and re-routes; 2 steps in 300 arrivals. Story/Skirmish untouched (trace-identical). |

| NET-01 | NOT NEEDED AT PRESENT (user, 2026-09-28: connected on two networks with no relay); filed for networks that cannot connect directly | No TURN relay/service credentials. Direct Google-STUN peer connections are implemented; success across restrictive NAT/firewalls is not guaranteed. This does not block solo or all co-op testing. **What a relay would take (report only, 2026-09-28; not built):** a TURN server that both browsers can reach and that forwards the game's data when no direct path exists. Either a small always-on machine running the free `coturn` (about 5 USD a month, a domain name, a TLS certificate and someone to keep it patched) or a managed TURN service billed by traffic (the game sends tens of kilobits per second, so the cost is small). The game is a static site and cannot keep a password secret, so it also needs a tiny endpoint that hands out short-lived TURN credentials; that endpoint is the same small server that short join codes (F.12) and lobbies (F.04) need. In the game: two more entries in `iceServers` and a line in the status text. Since Build 15 a failed direct connection is reported as such, so the need can be measured by how often players see that message. |

## 1. Movement stability — DONE (user acceptance 2026-09-23; M1.04 stairs/mantling DEFERRED by scope)

**Current ORIGINAL requirement:** sprint indefinitely while Shift + forward is active and existing movement/combat rules permit it. There is no stamina meter, exhaustion, recharge delay or stamina-based speed change. Preserve aiming, firing, reload, crouch, healing, story, four classes, bots, co-op and pointer controls. Do not add stairs, mantling or unrelated features. Code implementation is complete; the milestone remains UNVERIFIED until human camera/movement feel and supported live co-op acceptance are checked.

- [x] **M1.01 · DONE · ORIGINAL** — Implement unlimited sprint and fix the reproduced sprint/timing causes.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: T7 reproduced the former `>1` stamina threshold oscillation and `.05s` frame cap. E5/T8 remove all runtime resource dependencies and the meter/style/text, keep sprint active indefinitely, and process movement in bounded substeps. All four speed multipliers are unchanged; no stamina class perk existed to replace. T9 prevents substeps from backfilling automatic shots and preserves click/reload/cooldown behavior. This marks the confirmed code causes and requested removal complete, not a claim that every perceived jitter is eliminated.
- [x] **M1.02 · DONE · ORIGINAL/PLAN** — Verify camera stability and walk/sprint transitions.
  - **Accepted 2026-09-23 by user playtest:** "sprint jitter is gone in Safari on Build 04". Earlier the same day it was reopened: User observation, verbatim: "I played Build 04 in a real browser. Sprinting is fine for a few seconds, then I slow down and the view shakes." Diagnosis only so far: E10 lists candidate mechanisms and the F3 overlay (T10) records per-frame FPS, frame time, substeps, cap discard, render scale/pixel ratio, sprint gate, target and measured speed and FOV. Awaiting overlay numbers from a real sprint.
  - Earlier record: DONE code subpart: T8 checks release/restart, direction changes, combat gates, finite camera poses, equal-time FOV/weapon positions, bob settling and pause/reset. FOV/recoil and weapon roll/pitch use time-based damping; independent weapon base pose avoids accumulated offsets. Human feel, trackpad turning while sprinting, rendered frame pacing and live co-op motion/latency remain UNVERIFIED. No screen/audio recording was requested.
- [x] **M1.03 · DONE · ORIGINAL/PLAN** — Verify the scoped movement code: jumping, diagonal speed, walls, slopes and varying frame rates; stairs excluded.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: T8 passes 34 headless production-code checks, including 60 seconds at 15/30/60/120 FPS, all classes, diagonal normalization/turning, repeated jumps, existing wall stopping/sliding, ground slope, irregular frames, .1–.25s stalls, longer-stall cap, and host/guest pose/snapshot handling. T9 adds 24 combat-timing checks. Clear-lane endurance substitutes colliders; separate wall/slope checks use production geometry. These verify code behavior, not GPU FPS, an exhaustive corner/navigation sweep or actual WebRTC. Human acceptance remains M1.02/QA-01; remote jump visuals remain NET-02.

Human crouch feel stays in M1.02 acceptance.

Not built yet: they belong to the player-height build. Kohar Valley keeps its walls and props as tall as they are for movement, so that a real jump cannot change it.

## 2. Combat feel and feedback — TODO (Build 06: M2.03, M2.04 and M2.05 DONE; M2.01 hit-confirmation subpart done; M2.02 open)

- [ ] **M2.01 · TODO · ORIGINAL/PLAN** — Improve aiming, recoil, reload timing, muzzle effects, and hit confirmation.
  - Build 06 correction (user playtest): the kill-specific marker change is removed. One subtle neutral marker (26 px, off-white, light shadow) for any hit; headshots tint it red; kills add a small text-only "ENEMY DOWN" alert under the crosshair for 1.6 s with a plain .25 s opacity fade (no flashing or scaling), only for your own kills (guest via the `hit` reply). Reset clears marker and alert. T12.
  - Build 05 subpart DONE (code): hit confirmation. Two-tone `head` / single `confirm` triangle tick (respects the Audio toggle); marker enlarged (34px, bold, shadowed), red for headshots and scaled on kills, shown immediately. Guest gets the same via the extended `hit` reply (T11). Aiming, recoil, reload timing and muzzle polish untouched and still need playtest-driven work; weapon balance unchanged.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: E6/T1/T2/T5: ADS, recoil, fire cadence, magazine reloads, muzzle light/flash and hit marker exist; reload completion is verified. Further feel/timing polish needs playtesting; do not replace the working WeaponState.
- [ ] **M2.06 · TODO · ORIGINAL (user request 2026-09-23) · option 2 (hybrid ragdoll) chosen 2026-09-24, to be built after Ambush** — Ragdoll or hybrid physics death for characters (separate from breakable props).
  - **User decision (2026-09-24):** "I played Build 08 and the deaths still look the same to me. Authored variants are not achieving what I want." The user chose the **hybrid ragdoll, option 2** (authored directional reaction for .15–.3 s, then the in-house Verlet ragdoll; estimated 8–10 dev-days in E15), **to be built after Ambush mode (F.03)**. Options 1, 3 and 4 are not pursued. Open design questions for that task (from the investigation): collapse near the spot versus the push away from the shooter; acceptable limb differences between host and guest (the reaction is identical, the ragdoll may differ); bodies colliding with cover; whether the rifle drops.
  - Build 08: **option 0 implemented** (see the M2.04 Build 08 entry; T16). Kept: authored falls away from the shooter, no physics, bodies can still pass through cover (19% of falls in E15). Options 1–4 remain open.
  - Investigation only (E15). Options: **0** better variant selection without physics (hit-point offset and a per-kill value from the shared impact data, sideways falls) 2–3 dev-days, zero cost, identical on host and guest; **1** own Verlet particle ragdoll (15 particles, 37 constraints, terrain via `terrainAt`, walls via the 270 box occluders) 6–8 dev-days, ~0.12 ms/frame for 10 simultaneous bodies (headless), guest lands similarly but limbs can differ; **2 (recommended)** hybrid: authored directional reaction for .15–.3 s, then option 1 — 8–10 dev-days, the visible reaction identical on both peers; **3/4** cannon-es (346 KB) or Rapier (2.86 MB) 9–13 dev-days, heavier and still non-deterministic across peers unless the host sends the settled pose. Also fixes the 19% of falls that pass through cover.
  - Decisions needed: plausible collapse near the spot vs today's push away from the shooter; acceptable limb differences between host and guest; in-house solver vs library; bodies colliding with cover; rifle stays in hand or drops; whether AI turn rate/awareness may change (produces side/back kills naturally).
- [ ] **M2.07 · TODO · ORIGINAL (user request 2026-09-24) · not started** — **Wallbanging**: bullets penetrating thin cover (sandbags, timber, thin walls, props) with reduced damage. Existing: `hitScan` stops at the first occluder (`solidOccluders()`); `aiHit` has no ray at all (a bot hit strikes along the shooter–target line once the sight check passed); every solid is an unrotated box with no thickness or material data. Would need: a penetration table per material (which of the six prop materials and wall types are penetrable and at what damage factor), a second ray past the first hit to find the exit face and the target behind it, decals on both faces, the co-op `impact` message carrying a through-wall flag, and a decision whether enemies may fire at a player they cannot see (today fire permission needs sight; Ambush enemies always know where you are). Enemy hit chance and damage formulas stay unchanged unless explicitly requested.
- [ ] **M2.02 · TODO · PLAN** — Add clear damage direction and restrained, adjustable camera shake.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: E6: non-directional red vignette and recoil exist. No attacker-direction indicator or camera-shake adjustment.
- [x] **M2.03 · DONE · PLAN** — Add brief hit splatter and a blood-effects setting.
  - Build 06 (user playtest: "blood is too small… no blood on surfaces"): blood is now pooled in `dist/effects.js` (three fixed Points pools + one InstancedMesh; no per-hit allocation, constant draw calls, empty pools hidden). Body hit: 30 droplets + 6 back-spatter + 5 mist sprites (.62 m); headshot: 54 + 10 + 7 larger sprites (1.0 m); sideways spread .75–.93 m (body) and 1.3–1.8 m (head) after .25 s, vs ~.3 m in Build 05. Emitted 15 cm toward the shooter so the torso does not hide it; dark linear colours so it does not glow; sprite size follows zoom. Persistent decals: a wall/solid up to 2.5 m behind along the shot, else the floor 0.35–1.15 m behind (higher of the exact terrain triangle and any floor/table/cover top); oriented to the surface, clamped inside box faces, never on surfaces buried under the ground; head decals larger; cap **40**, oldest retired first; cleared by mission reset and the menu. Every character hit including the human players leaves the same decal on both peers via the co-op `impact` message. The Blood checkbox suppresses spray and decals at spawn and hides existing ones when turned off. T12, B1; browser screenshots checked at 6 m and 15–40 m (Chromium pane). Safari appearance unverified.
  - Build 05: every character hit (players and ally bots on enemies, enemies on allies, the co-op teammate avatar; not the local first-person view) spawns a dark mist (.24s) and a 10-droplet gravity spray (.35s), both disposed by the existing effect loop; cosmetic randomness uses `Math.random`, not the seeded gameplay RNG. "Blood" checkbox beside Audio, on by default, read at spawn time for local and replayed hits. T11 verifies spawning, suppression, co-op replay and disposal; appearance is not yet playtested.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: E6: surface-impact dust exists; no character blood splatter or blood setting.
- [x] **M2.04 · DONE · ORIGINAL/PLAN** — Add directional hit reactions and natural falling/death animations or supported ragdolls.
  - Build 08 (M2.06 option 0; Build 06 playtest: "the six death variants still feel practically identical"): the choice also uses the hit height on the torso, how far off-centre the hit is and a per-kill value from the hit point (deterministic; neither `Math.random` nor the seeded `rand()`). Head: collapse, else stagger (front), pitch (behind) or spin (side). Side: spin 70 / collapse 30%. Behind: pitch 75 / spin 25%. Shoulder/arm (>0.18 m off-centre): spin 60 / stagger 40%. Front chest high: stagger 45 / collapse 30 / spin 25%. Front chest low (≤1.25 m, where centre-mass and bot hits land): doubleover 40 / stagger 35 / collapse 25%. Abdomen: doubleover 70 / crumple 30%. Legs: crumple. The host sends the variant (`dv`) and strength (`st`) in the impact, so the guest plays the same death. Measured over 600 face-on kills (the case the Build 06/07 AI produces): stagger 38 / doubleover 34 / collapse 25 / spin 3%, where Build 07 played stagger 100%. Bot kills: stagger 70% → doubleover 39% at most. T16.
  - Build 06 (user playtest: "every death plays the same animation"): six authored variants chosen deterministically by body location and shot direction relative to the victim's facing: headshot → **collapse** (legs give way), front chest → **stagger** (steps back, arms up, falls backwards), chest from behind → **pitch** (buckles, falls forward), chest from the side → **spin** (turned by the impact), abdomen → **doubleover**, legs → **crumple** (drops to the knees first). All tilt toward the shot direction, so the body always ends away from the shooter; durations .75–.95 s. As the body reaches the ground it settles into a flat rest pose lifted by its thickness on the grounded side, so limbs and rifle stay on the ground (Build 05 sank up to 0.77 m). Hit zones: head > 1.45 m (unchanged headshot rule), upper > 1.0, lower > .72, legs. Guests receive zone and victim yaw in the impact. No physics or ragdoll. T12.
  - Build 05 (`characters.js` `react`/`reset`/`state`): non-lethal hits push the torso and rig away from the shooter for .22s, visual only (outer transform, AI and hitboxes unaffected). Kills play an authored fall: the rig pivots at the feet toward the shot direction with accelerating rotation over .62s, a slide of .22–.60m (stronger for headshots), and a .2s settle bounce; the instant π/2 roll and +.2m lift are removed. Bot hits use chest height and shooter→target direction, never headshots. Allies still stand up instantly at the 15s regroup (unchanged). No physics/ragdoll. T11 verifies direction, timing, completion, co-op replay and snapshot non-interference; visual quality needs playtest.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: E6/E7: limbs blend toward a downed pose, but game logic immediately rolls the whole actor by π/2. No directional hit reaction, momentum fall or ragdoll.
- [x] **M2.05 · DONE · PLAN** — Bound body/effect lifetimes and check performance.
  - Build 08: enemy bodies stay shootable (blood and a decal only; no score, kill, health or mission effect) and sink into the ground over 1.5 s after 40 s, or at once (oldest first) when more than 5 are lying; a sunk body is hidden and reused by reinforcements. The guest follows the host's `gn` mask. No allocation per despawn; 0 geometries/materials over three cycles (T16). All seven enemies on screen cost 8.7–9.2 ms per frame in Chromium on the M1 (B3).
  - Build 06: measured (B1) — blood + decals cost 0.03–0.07 ms per frame of GPU/whole-frame time on the user's M1 at 8.6–47.6 hits/s, +4 draw calls total, no per-hit allocation, no recompile hitch; lifetimes bounded (particles ≤ 1 s, decals capped at 40, bodies bounded by 7 enemies until reset). Limits: Chromium pane, not Safari; no long-session soak. The dominant combat frame cost is PERF-02 (terrain raycasts), not effects.
  - Build 05 subpart: blood effects are bounded (.24s/.35s) and T11 shows no geometry/material or scene growth over 5 × 7 kills with reset. GPU cost of blood under sustained automatic fire and long-session memory remain unmeasured.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: E6: tracers expire after .05s and dust after .32s; expired effect geometry/materials are disposed and reset clears them. Enemy bodies remain until reset but count is bounded by seven enemies. No long-session memory/performance measurement.

Required regardless: make props hideable (instancing or per-prop vertex ranges instead of `batchStatic` merging), world restore in `reset()`, a co-op break message + snapshot bitmask, shadow refresh policy. "Containers" (barrels, jars) do not exist yet and would be new art.
  - Decisions needed before building: which props break; what breaks them (player bullets only? hits to break?); whether broken cover still blocks movement or LOS; contents cosmetic only (recommended) or pickups; how long contents persist; shadow refresh vs stale shadows; rendering approach.

Decisions needed before building: may steep ground block movement (an invisible wall is "visibly fake"), and which areas may change (the Ambush arena stays the west district; the relay approach and extraction routes are mission-critical).

    - First wave at 20 s [25]; waves 22–32 s apart [single spawns 14–22 s apart].
    - A wave is trimmed to the remaining budget and alive cap. The recycling pool is sunk corpses first, then up to 3 old unseen ones.
    - Measured (T16, each wave cleared 12 s after arrival): stage 0 waves 2/2/2 at 20/52/80 s; stage 1 3/3/3 at 20/53/80 s; skirmish 3/3/3/3 at 20/48/73/101 s.
    - Accuracy, damage and fire rate unchanged (fire block and `aiHit` byte-identical, T13); at most 3 shooters per target. Relay capture and skirmish still complete (T13).
    - A spacing bug in the first wave draft (two members on one spot) was found while writing T16 and fixed before commit.

At most 3 enemies shoot at one target. Fire cadence, hit chance and damage are byte-identical to Build 06. Reinforcements recycle corpses nobody can see to hidden entry points ≥35 m from humans, per-stage budget 4/6/3 (skirmish 8), alive cap 5/6/4 (skirmish 6), first after 25 s and a death, then every 14–22 s; non-garrison enemies leave the relay area during capture and a hidden garrison enemy stands up after 12 s, so capture always completes. All values are live tunables in `ENEMY_AI`. AI decisions use a separate seeded stream. T13; human feel and difficulty UNVERIFIED.
  - Existing / verification / remaining: Inspected `game.js` `reset`/`tickAI`: seven enemies spawn once at fixed points in `reset()`, never respawn after death, and idle-wander to a random point within ±4m of their spawn every 6–11s; on sighting a target they stop and fire in place. No spawner, wave pacing, wider patrol routes or tactical repositioning. Not implemented; must respect story pacing, relay capture rules, co-op host authority and performance. Overlaps M8 wave work; decide the relationship when this is scheduled.
